===== ICLE-US-IND-0001.1.txt =====
Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay shows strong personal engagement with the topic and a clear moral position. You care deeply about environmental damage and corporate responsibility, and that passion gives your writing energy. You also use a well-known historical example, which is a good strategy in argumentative writing. To revise successfully, focus on clarity of argument, formal academic tone, grammar, and organization.

Strengths

Clear topic: You argue that corporate greed and negligence can cause great harm.
Relevant example: The Exxon Valdez spill is a strong case for discussing environmental responsibility.
Emotional force: Your concern for wildlife, native communities, and the environment is persuasive.
Conclusion with a call to action: You end by asking for accountability and better environmental protection, which fits the purpose of an argumentative essay.

Main problems to revise

A. Make your thesis more precise
Your opening begins with “Money has been said to be the root of all evil,” but the essay is really about corporate negligence and environmental accountability, not money in general.

A stronger thesis would be more specific, for example:

> The Exxon Valdez disaster shows that large corporations must be held accountable for environmental damage caused by negligence, poor safety planning, and slow emergency response.

This thesis gives your essay a clearer focus.

B. Improve organization
Right now, some ideas are repeated, and the essay moves between personal reaction, facts, and opinion without a clear structure. Try this structure:

Introduction
   - Brief background on the Exxon Valdez spill
   - Clear thesis

Body Paragraph 1: The scale of the disaster
   - What happened
   - Effects on wildlife, local people, and the environment

Body Paragraph 2: Exxon's responsibility
   - Slow response
   - Safety failures
   - Evidence that better maintenance or planning might have prevented the spill

Body Paragraph 3: Why accountability matters
   - Compensation
   - Research funding
   - Stronger environmental regulation

Conclusion
   - Restate your main argument
   - Emphasize the need for corporate responsibility

C. Use a more academic tone
Some phrases are too emotional or informal for academic writing. For example:

“catastrophe beyond belief”
“mind boggling”
“I loathed the corporation”
“pollute and rape our land”

These expressions are powerful, but in formal writing they can sound exaggerated. Try more neutral language:

“a severe environmental disaster”
“enormous financial resources”
“I strongly criticized the corporation”
“seriously damage the environment”

You can still be persuasive without sounding overly emotional.

Language and grammar issues

A. Word choice and collocations
Some expressions are not natural in English:

“personally true” → true for me / something I believe
“witnessed first hand” → witnessed firsthand
“issues of big business” → the problems caused by large corporations
“made monetary amends” → provided financial compensation
“set aside monies” → set aside funds

B. Sentence structure
Some sentences are awkward or incomplete. For example:

> “Prior to the grounding the Exxon, many wildlife organizations…”

This should be revised to:

> Before the Exxon Valdez ran aground, many wildlife organizations and concerned citizens asked Exxon an important question: how would the company prevent such a catastrophe?

Also, this part has a serious problem:

> “Each time Exxon was confronted with this question, spokespersons said the same thing. .”

There is missing content here. You need either to complete it with a quotation or remove it.

C. Grammar corrections
Watch for these issues:

Articles: “in Prince William sound” → in Prince William Sound
Prepositions: “Captain in charge the vessel” → captain in charge of the vessel
Capitalization: “sound” should be Sound
Comparison structure: “such a profitable corporation such as Exxon” → such a profitable corporation as Exxon
Subject-verb agreement: “The publishing of Exxon's notes and court transcriptions, prove…” → The publication of Exxon's notes and court transcripts proves…

Development of evidence
Your essay would be stronger if you included more specific factual support. For example:

How much oil was spilled?
What were the effects on fishing and wildlife?
Why was Exxon's response considered slow?
What evidence shows poor maintenance?

Be careful to distinguish between personal reaction and evidence. In academic argument, evidence is more convincing than emotion alone.

Final advice for revision
When revising, focus on these priorities:

Write a clear thesis about corporate responsibility.
Reorganize the essay into logical paragraphs.
Replace highly emotional phrases with a more formal academic tone.
Correct grammar and awkward wording.
Add specific evidence to support your claims.

You already have a strong opinion and a meaningful topic. With clearer structure and more formal language, your essay can become much more persuasive and effective.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0002.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: crime may bring money for a short time, but in the end it destroys lives. This is a strong argumentative idea, and your use of a real-life example makes the essay more convincing and emotionally powerful. You also keep your focus on the main point throughout the essay, which is good.

To revise effectively, focus on organization, grammar, word choice, and academic style.

Main strengths
Clear argument: Your thesis is understandable—crime does not truly pay.
Relevant example: The story of the businessman supports your point well.
Consistent focus: Most sentences connect to the topic of illegal ways of getting rich.

Organization and development
Your essay has an introduction, a body example, and a conclusion, but the structure can be clearer.

Problems
The introduction is too broad and has several unclear sentences.
The thesis is not stated smoothly because the sentence ends with “as the title of this essay states .”
The second paragraph tells a story, but it is very long and could be better organized.
The conclusion repeats ideas but could explain the lesson more strongly.

Suggestions
Try this structure:
Introduction: Explain why some people choose crime and clearly state your thesis.
Body paragraph: Give the businessman example.
Analysis paragraph: Explain exactly how his crime harmed him and his family.
Conclusion: Restate the main argument and final lesson.

A possible revised thesis:
Although some people believe crime is a quick way to become rich, illegal wealth is temporary and usually leads to punishment, family suffering, and personal ruin.

Grammar and sentence problems
There are many grammar errors that make some ideas hard to understand. Pay special attention to:

A. Sentence structure
Example:
Original: “Most of the people that turn to crime are either fed up with societies rules for becoming rich and famous.”
Problem: “Either” suggests two choices, but the sentence does not finish the comparison correctly.
Revision: “Many people turn to crime because they are frustrated with society’s rules about becoming rich and successful.”

B. Word form and spelling
Some common errors:
societies rules → society’s rules
expectable → acceptable or normal
see and easier way → see an easier way
catigory → category
will → willing
beleive → believe
families money → family’s money
way very proud → was very proud

C. Articles and plurals
Examples:
the old couples money → the old couples’ money
a fortune 500 company → a Fortune 500 company

D. Verb tense and agreement
Example:
“There was no reason for this man to have chosen…” is acceptable, but simpler is better:
  “There was no reason for this man to choose the get-rich-quick path.”

Style and academic tone
Your essay sounds personal and conversational in places. That is not always wrong, but for academic writing, use a more formal tone.

Avoid informal phrases such as:
“The bottom line is…”
“get rich quick attitude”

You can replace them with:
Ultimately
In conclusion
the desire for quick wealth
an illegal shortcut to success

Also, “I know several people…” is interesting, but if you use personal examples, explain them clearly and formally.

Coherence and clarity
Some transitions are weak or missing. Use linking words to guide the reader:
For example
As a result
However
In the end
Therefore

Example revision:
For a short time, the man appeared successful. However, his wealth was based on fraud. As a result, he was arrested, his assets were taken, and his family lost both financial security and trust.

Punctuation
You have several comma problems:
Do not put commas between a subject and verb.
Be careful with sentence fragments.

Example:
Original: “Crime does not pay, is a very true statement.”
Revision: “The statement ‘Crime does not pay’ is very true.”

Suggested revised sentence examples
Here are a few possible improvements:

Original: “Some people are not patient and see and easier way of earning money…”
Revised: “Some people lack patience and choose what seems like an easier way to make money, such as theft or drug dealing.”

Original: “The government took control of all the families money…”
Revised: “The government seized the family’s assets, including the house they lived in.”

Original: “The bottom line is that crime does not pay and that it destroys your life.”
Revised: “In the end, crime does not provide real success; instead, it ruins lives and destroys families.”

Final advice
For your next draft, focus on:
Writing a clear thesis
Dividing long paragraphs into smaller ones
Correcting grammar and spelling carefully
Using a more formal academic tone
Explaining how your example proves your argument

You have a strong central message and a meaningful example. With clearer grammar and more formal organization, this essay can become much more persuasive.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0003.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear intellectual goal: you argue that pay inequality is not only about the difficulty of work, but about how society assigns value to different kinds of labor. The example of child care is effective because it shows a real contradiction in American society: people say children matter, but child care workers are still underpaid. This is a strong and thoughtful central idea.

What you do well

Strong topic and critical thinking  
   You move beyond a simple “equal work deserves equal pay” argument and examine deeper cultural values. This gives the essay sophistication.

Good use of example  
   The child care example is relevant and persuasive. It helps make the abstract idea of “social value” more concrete.

Academic tone  
   Your vocabulary and sentence style sound formal and appropriate for argumentative writing.

Main areas to revise

Make the thesis clearer
The first sentence is incomplete: “The idea that  seems at first…” This makes the introduction confusing. Your reader needs a clear thesis early in the essay.

Try stating your claim more directly. For example:

Although Americans claim to believe in equality, wages are often determined not by the true social importance of work but by cultural assumptions about value.
Child care workers are underpaid because American society does not fully recognize their contribution, even though their work is essential to the economy.

A strong thesis will help unify the whole essay.

Improve organization and progression
Your essay has good ideas, but some sentences are very long and contain several ideas at once. This makes the logic harder to follow.

A clearer structure could be:

Paragraph 1: Introduce the argument about equality and social value.
Paragraph 2: Explain the contradiction using child care work.
Paragraph 3: Show why child care is economically essential and discuss possible solutions or complications.
Conclusion: Restate the main point clearly.

Also, make sure each paragraph begins with a topic sentence that signals its main idea.

Simplify long sentences
Some sentences are grammatically correct in parts but difficult to read because they are overloaded.

For example:

> “It is difficult to imagine, however, a society where workers are encouraged to take employment seriously... without considering the fundamental value of child care providers.”

This idea is good, but it can be made clearer:

It is difficult to imagine a strong society without recognizing the fundamental value of child care providers.

In academic writing, clarity is more important than complexity.

Grammar and word choice corrections
There are several small language issues. Here are important ones:

“profess to the belief” → “profess the belief” or “claim to believe”
“a pereniel subject and topic” → “a perennial subject of debate”
“man with family responsibilities” → “men with family responsibilities”
“a work force” → usually “a workforce”
“contribution to the society they live in” → “contribution to the society in which they live” or simply “contribution to society”
Remove unnecessary spaces, for example in “without  involving”

Also watch article use and plural forms, which are common challenges for Chinese speakers.

Strengthen the argument with more precise evidence
Right now, the essay is mostly theoretical. Your reasoning is strong, but it would be more persuasive with specific support. For example, you could include:

the low wages of child care workers compared with teachers or other care-related professions
the number of working parents who depend on child care
the role of child care in supporting the broader economy

Even one or two concrete facts would strengthen your credibility.

Clarify your conclusion
Your final sentence has an interesting idea, but it is slightly hard to interpret. You seem to mean that society judges work as valuable only when it is well paid, even though this is unfair. Make that point more directly.

For example:

The central irony is that society often uses income as proof of value; therefore, until child care workers are paid more fairly, their contribution will continue to be underestimated.

Sentence-level revision example

Original:
> “If we consider that our society rewards valuable labor - valuable contributions - with first, economic measures, and, second, measures of status, then the child care worker may reasonably be judged as not providing a ‘valuable’ contribution to society.”

Possible revision:
> If society rewards important work with higher pay and greater status, then the low wages of child care workers suggest that their contribution is not fully valued.

This version is shorter and clearer but keeps your meaning.

Final advice
Your essay shows strong ideas and a serious academic voice. The main revision goals are:
write a complete, direct thesis
shorten and clarify long sentences
improve grammar and word choice
add concrete evidence
make the conclusion more explicit

You already have a thoughtful argument. With clearer language and structure, your essay will become much more persuasive.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0004.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay presents a clear and important argument: unpaid domestic labor, especially homemaking and raising children, should be recognized as socially valuable. The topic is meaningful, and your essay shows strong engagement with social values, gender roles, and economic fairness. You also use a formal tone and some effective transitions such as “However,” “Therefore,” and “On the other hand.” These are good strengths to build on.

Main strengths

Clear position:  
Your thesis is easy to identify. You argue that society wrongly measures human worth by income and should recognize homemaking labor more fully.

Good organization at the paragraph level:  
The essay moves from the problem, to examples of paid work, to the difficulty of comparing paid and unpaid labor, and finally to a possible solution.

Thought-provoking ideas:  
Your point that market value is not the same as human value is insightful. The discussion of stay-at-home parents feeling ashamed is also persuasive.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction presents two slightly different claims:
Homemaking is as valuable as paid work.
Homemakers should receive financial compensation.

These are related, but not identical. Decide what your essay mainly argues. If your main claim is about financial payment, then the body should focus more on why and how society should pay homemakers. If your claim is about social recognition, then the conclusion should not suddenly emphasize paychecks.

B. Develop the argument with more evidence
Right now, many claims are general. For example:
“those at-home tasks contribute services to society equally valuable”
“many of the ills of society… have been traced”
“the media makes her feel behind the times”

These statements need support. In revision, add:
specific examples of unpaid labor: childcare, cooking, elder care, emotional support, household management
evidence or reasoning about economic value
more explanation of why payment would improve fairness or social respect

Even if you do not use research sources, you can explain your reasoning more fully.

C. Avoid overgeneralization
Some phrases sound too broad or outdated, such as:
“first-world cultures”
“women exclusively”
“doctors, lawyers and Indian chiefs”
“the at-home female”

These expressions may weaken your credibility because they sound stereotypical or old-fashioned. Use more neutral and inclusive language, such as:
“many developed societies”
“traditionally performed by women”
“stay-at-home parent”
“paid professions”

D. Strengthen coherence
Some paragraphs shift focus. For example, paragraph 4 discusses both social shame and media pressure, but the connection to compensation is not fully explained. Make sure each paragraph begins with a clear topic sentence and ends by linking back to your thesis.

Language issues to revise

Grammar and word choice
There are several sentence-level problems. Here are important examples:

“the dollars he or she generates”  
  Better: “the income he or she earns”

“equated with worth as a person”  
  Better: “equated with a person’s worth”

“compensated by the dollar”  
  Better: “financially compensated”

“for he or she who works outside the home”  
  Better: “for those who work outside the home”

“an neurosurgeon”  
  Correct: “a neurosurgeon”

“this contribution to society in so immense”  
  Correct: “this contribution to society is so immense”

“whether it’s Mom or Dad”  
  In a formal essay, better: “whether it is the mother or the father” or “whether either parent stays at home”

“if it, of course, the worker is married”  
  Correct: “if, of course, the worker is married”

Style
Some sentences are too long and difficult to follow. Try dividing long sentences into two shorter ones. This will improve clarity.

For example, your first sentence is interesting but very long. You could split it:
“In many developed societies, a person’s value is often measured by the income he or she earns. Unfortunately, financial reward has become closely associated with personal worth.”

Suggestions for revision

Here is one possible structure:

Introduction
Explain the social problem: society values paid work more than unpaid domestic work.
End with a clear thesis: for example, “Because homemaking and caregiving are essential to society, they should receive greater social recognition and some form of economic compensation.”

Body Paragraph 1
Explain what homemaking includes and why it is socially necessary.

Body Paragraph 2
Show why market salary is not a fair measure of human contribution.

Body Paragraph 3
Discuss possible forms of recognition: direct payment, tax benefits, family income-sharing, retirement credits, etc

Conclusion
Restate the argument and explain why recognizing unpaid labor benefits society as a whole.

Final advice

You have a strong central idea and a serious academic tone. Your next step is to make the essay more precise, more supported, and more modern in language. Focus especially on:
clarifying the thesis,
adding specific evidence and examples,
revising outdated or overly broad wording,
correcting grammar and sentence structure.

This essay has a solid foundation, and with revision, it can become much more convincing.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0005.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a strong intellectual ambition. You are not simply agreeing or disagreeing with the saying “money is the root of all evil”; instead, you question whether the saying is logically coherent and whether ideas like *desire*, *virtue*, *evil*, and *happiness* can be measured at all. This is thoughtful and original. Your vocabulary is advanced, and your tone is serious and philosophical.

To revise effectively, focus on clarity, structure, and language accuracy.

Main strengths
Original argument: You challenge the proverb rather than treating it literally.
Sophisticated thinking: You recognize that the problem lies in defining and measuring “desire” and “evil.”
Ambitious style: Your essay aims for a mature, analytical voice.

Main problems to revise

A. Your thesis is not clear enough
Your central claim seems to be: the saying is meaningless or impractical because there is no reliable way to connect money, desire, virtue, and evil objectively. This is a strong thesis, but it appears only after several dense sentences.

Advice: State your main claim earlier and more directly.  
For example:

> The proverb “money is the root of all evil” is misleading because money itself cannot be measured against moral qualities such as desire, virtue, or evil.

This will help the reader follow your reasoning.

B. Paragraph organization needs improvement
Right now, each paragraph contains interesting ideas, but the logical progression is hard to follow. Some sentences feel abstract before the key point is established.

A possible structure:
Introduction: Present the proverb and your thesis.
Paragraph 1: Explain why poverty does not automatically produce virtue.
Paragraph 2: Discuss why desire for money cannot be objectively measured.
Paragraph 3: Explain the relationship among happiness, wealth, and virtue.
Conclusion: Use history to show that wealth and power can be used for either good or evil.

Each paragraph should begin with a clear topic sentence.

C. Some ideas are too abstract
You use many concepts—*evil, virtue, desire, happiness*—but often without precise definition. This makes the argument sound impressive, but sometimes unclear.

For example, this sentence is difficult:

> The desire for money would-must- coincide and flourish with a desire for virtue if virtue, here defined as the counterpart of "happiness", is to thrive.

The reader may not understand exactly how happiness, virtue, and money are linked.

Advice: Use simpler explanation and perhaps one concrete example. For instance, you could mention a wealthy philanthropist and a corrupt ruler to show that money does not determine morality.

Language issues

A. Grammar and word choice
There are several errors in spelling and usage:
privelaged → *privileged*
acruement → *accrual* or *accumulation*
wheighed → *weighed*
serrendipitously → *serendipitously*
a least → *at least*
where in which → choose one: *where* or *in which*

Also check verb forms:
If money was the root of all evil → better: *If money were the root of all evil*  
  (“were” is standard in hypothetical statements)

B. Sentence control
Many sentences are too long and overloaded with clauses, dashes, and interruptions. This makes your argument harder to understand.

For example:

> I contend that the saying is meaningless-or a least impractical - for summing up the woes and corruption of humanity since desire-much less evil- cannot be appropriated objectively and wheighed against financial aptitude.

This could be revised as:

> I contend that the saying is at least impractical as an explanation of human corruption. Desire, and even more so evil, cannot be measured objectively against wealth.

Shorter sentences will make your ideas more powerful.

C. Punctuation and formatting
Use dashes carefully; too many make the prose feel interrupted.
Put spaces correctly around punctuation.
The symbol  should be removed and replaced with a proper transition or sentence.

Style advice for a non-native writer
Because your first language is Chinese, one likely challenge is that in English academic writing, clarity is usually valued more than rhetorical density. A sentence may sound more formal if it is long, but in English it often sounds stronger if it is direct.

Try this rule:
One sentence = one main idea
One paragraph = one stage of the argument

Revision priorities
When you rewrite, do these in order:
Write a clear thesis in the introduction.
Reorganize paragraphs so each has one clear purpose.
Simplify long sentences.
Define key terms more clearly.
Correct spelling, grammar, and punctuation.

Final comment
This essay shows strong potential. You clearly think deeply and are willing to engage with moral and philosophical questions. Your next step is to make your argument easier for the reader to follow. If you combine your intellectual ambition with clearer structure and simpler sentence style, the essay will become much more persuasive.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0006.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay raises an interesting question about Marx’s principle and whether it can work in Western society. You show strong critical thinking by considering family, religion, freedom, and moral relativism. The essay also has an academic tone and attempts to move from abstract theory to practical examples. For revision, focus on clarity of thesis, logical organization, and sentence-level accuracy.

Main strengths
Ambitious topic: You connect Marx, family structure, Christianity, and individualism.
Analytical approach: You do not simply describe the idea; you question its practicality.
Use of examples: Family and Western religion help make the discussion concrete.

Main issue: unclear thesis
Your position appears only gradually. By the end, you seem to argue that Marx’s ideal is admirable but not realistic in Western culture because of individual freedom and moral relativism. This should be stated clearly in the introduction.

Suggested revision strategy
In the introduction, state:
what Marx’s principle is,
what your essay will argue,
the main reasons.

For example:
> Although Marx’s ideal of equality reflects values already present in families and religious traditions, it is difficult to apply in modern Western society because individuals define contribution differently, personal freedom resists external control, and moral relativism weakens shared ethical standards.

Organization and development
The essay has good ideas, but some paragraphs do not connect smoothly.

Problems
The essay begins with family and Christianity as support for egalitarianism, but later turns to arguments against applying Marx’s principle. The shift needs clearer transitions.
Some points are underdeveloped. For example, the paragraph about affluent society introduces “giving back” and reward, but the connection to Marx’s principle is not fully explained.
The conclusion is too brief compared with the complexity of the essay.

How to improve structure
A clearer structure might be:
Introduction: explain Marx’s principle and your thesis.
Paragraph 1: show where egalitarian values already exist (family).
Paragraph 2: show how religion/social institutions support similar values.
Paragraph 3: explain why applying this principle to society is difficult—different views of contribution and reward.
Paragraph 4: discuss individual freedom.
Paragraph 5: discuss moral relativism.
Conclusion: restate your argument and explain why the ideal remains admirable but impractical.

Use of evidence and precision
Some claims are too broad, for example:
“In Western society, the primary guide is Christianity.”
“The modern person lives in a world where relativism reigns supreme.”

These are strong generalizations. In academic writing, qualify them more carefully:
“Historically, Christianity has been a major moral influence in many Western societies.”
“In many modern societies, shared moral standards are less stable than in the past.”

Also, the placeholders  must be replaced. If you quote Marx or the New Testament, provide the exact wording and source if required.

Language issues to revise

Grammar and wording
A few examples:

“on e would understand” → “one would understand”
“social institutions & practices in order which preserve”  
  Better: “social institutions and practices that preserve”
“western ideology” → “Western ideology”  
  (“Western” should be capitalized)
“who it to define” → “who is to define”
“The  religious” → “The religious”
“form the family” → “from the family”
“will unlikely be achieved” → “is unlikely to be achieved”

Style
Avoid “&” in formal academic writing. Use and.

Word choice
Some expressions sound unnatural:
“Absent a deviation from our general nature…” is grammatically possible but heavy and unclear.  
  Simpler: “Because humans naturally seek to protect their offspring, egalitarian care is a natural part of family life.”
“The individual is paramount in the post-modern world.”  
  Better: “In the postmodern world, individual autonomy is often treated as a central value.”

Tone and argumentation
Your rhetorical questions are effective, but too many can weaken academic authority. Instead of asking several questions in a row, turn some into direct claims.

For example:
> Because people define valuable contribution differently, it is difficult to create a fair system of reward. Raising responsible children may be as beneficial to society as public activism, yet such contributions are hard to compare.

This sounds stronger and more academic.

Final advice
When revising, focus on:
making your thesis explicit,
improving paragraph order and transitions,
supporting broad claims with careful wording,
correcting grammar and formal style.

You have the basis for a thoughtful argumentative essay. Your central insight—that egalitarian ideals may be morally attractive but difficult to enforce in individualistic Western societies—is strong. If you present that argument more clearly and precisely, the essay will become much more persuasive.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0007.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear central idea: whether crime “pays” depends on how we define “pay,” and the answer should include not only money but also time, health, and legal consequences. This is a strong argumentative direction. The essay is organized logically, and each paragraph develops a different kind of “cost.” Your examples also help make the discussion concrete.

Strengths

Clear focus
   - You stay on the topic throughout the essay.
   - The essay consistently returns to the key question: what counts as “pay”?

Logical structure
   - The introduction defines the problem.
   - The body paragraphs examine different categories of loss: money, time, health, and legal issues.
   - The conclusion returns to the main question.

Effective use of examples
   - The opening jewelry store example is vivid and easy to understand.
   - This helps readers follow your reasoning.

Main areas to improve

Make your thesis more direct
Your position is somewhat delayed. In argumentative writing, it is better to state your claim clearly near the end of the introduction.

For example, you could revise your thesis to something like:

> Crime rarely pays because any short-term financial gain is usually outweighed by losses in time, money, health, and legal freedom.

This makes your argument more forceful and easier for the reader to follow.

Improve paragraph unity and topic sentences
Some paragraphs begin generally and then move into several ideas. Try to make each paragraph start with a clear topic sentence.

For example:

Current: “One issue to consider as a loss is the type of capital loss illustrated above.”
Stronger: “The first reason crime often does not pay is that it requires significant financial and time investment.”

This tells the reader exactly what the paragraph will argue.

Use a more formal academic tone
At times, the essay sounds conversational or informal, especially with phrases like:
“dollars and cents”
“small businessman criminal”
“does better the legitimate businessman”
“no taxes”

A more formal tone would strengthen the essay. For example:
“simple financial accounting” instead of “dollars and cents accounting”
“individual criminal” instead of “small businessman criminal”

Also, the final sentence sounds humorous, but it weakens the seriousness of the conclusion. In academic argument, it is usually better to end with a strong summary rather than a joke.

Watch word choice and collocations
There are several vocabulary and expression problems that seem influenced by non-native phrasing.

Examples:
“all manners of loss” → better: “all kinds of losses”
“enacting of a crime” → better: “carrying out a crime”
“equiptment” → spelling: “equipment”
“monies earned” → better: “money earned” or “profits”
“health related loss” → better: “health-related loss”
“lessen both a person's life span” → better: “shorten a person’s life span”
“does better the legitimate businessman” → should be “does better than the legitimate businessman”

Be careful with grammar and sentence structure
There are some grammar issues, especially with articles, parallel structure, and comparison.

Examples:
“the planning and enacting of a crime”
  - Better parallel form: “the planning and execution of a crime”
“then crimes can earn less to justify time spent”
  - This is unclear. Better: “then a smaller illegal profit may seem sufficient to justify the time spent.”
“Another type of loss to be considered is loss of mental or physical health and well being.”
  - Better: “Another type of loss is the decline in mental and physical health and well-being.”

Strengthen the conclusion
Your conclusion should clearly restate your argument and leave the reader with your final judgment. Right now, the conclusion becomes too focused on the comparison with business.

A stronger conclusion might be:

> In conclusion, crime may produce short-term financial gain in some cases, but once time, physical danger, emotional stress, and legal consequences are considered, it usually does not pay.

Smaller corrections

Use “getaway cars” rather than “get-away cars”
Use “well-being” with a hyphen
Consider gender-neutral language instead of repeated “his”
  - For example: “their crime,” “the criminal,” or “a person”

Final advice for revision

When revising, focus on these three priorities:

Add a clear thesis in the introduction.
Make each paragraph begin with a focused topic sentence.
Edit for formal academic language, grammar, and word choice.

Overall, this is a thoughtful essay with a strong underlying argument. Your ideas are clear and interesting; now your main task is to make the language more precise and academic.

===== ICLE-US-IND-0008.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay presents a clear position: money itself is not evil, but the love of money can lead to harmful behavior. This is a strong and arguable thesis, and your writing shows good control of abstract ideas. You also use examples from family, society, and nations, which helps broaden the discussion. For revision, focus on organization, precision, grammar, and style.

Main strengths

Clear argument
Your central claim is consistent throughout the essay.
You make an important distinction between money and the love of money, which gives the essay depth.

Serious tone
The essay sounds thoughtful and persuasive.
Expressions like “Our society pays dearly” and “wreaks havoc” create emphasis.

Range of examples
You discuss individuals, families, companies, and nations.
This helps show that the issue is universal.

Main areas to improve

A. Create a clearer essay structure
Right now, the essay reads more like a series of reflections than a fully organized argumentative essay. Try this structure:

Introduction
   - Introduce the topic.
   - State your thesis clearly.

Body Paragraph 1: Why money is necessary
   - Explain practical functions of money.

Body Paragraph 2: Why the love of money is dangerous
   - Explain greed and its consequences.

Body Paragraph 3: Examples
   - Give specific examples from society or personal life.

Conclusion
   - Restate your argument in a concise way.

Your current first sentence, “This idea is completely erroneous,” is too vague because the reader does not yet know what “this idea” refers to. Name the idea directly.

Possible revision:
“The statement that money is the root of all evil is incorrect. Money is a necessary part of daily life; rather, it is the excessive love of money that causes many social and moral problems.”

B. Use more precise and formal academic language
Some wording is effective but slightly informal or too emotional for an academic essay.

Examples:
“How many times have we seen on the evening news…” → too conversational
“the ‘right’ car” is okay, but repeated quotation marks reduce formality
“It is a cross-cultural thing” → too informal and vague

Better options:
“News reports frequently show…”
“This problem occurs across cultures.”
“Many people mistakenly believe that wealth, status, and material possessions will bring happiness.”

C. Improve grammar and sentence correctness
There are several sentence-level errors. Here are important ones:

Punctuation
“Money perhaps, is a necessary evil…”  
  → “Money, perhaps, is a necessary evil…”
“This is a different concept all together.”  
  → “This is an altogether different concept.”
“However, the love it…”  
  → “However, the love of it…”

Capitalization
“he begins to hide it…”  
  → “He begins to hide it…”

Word choice / spelling
“capitivates” → captivates
“sued for good” → probably used for good
“it’s needs met” → its needs met

Sentence fragments
“A healthy thing can be turned into a tool for disaster. is money the root of all evil?”  
  The second sentence should begin with a capital letter:  
  → “A healthy thing can be turned into a tool for disaster. Is money the root of all evil?”

D. Avoid overgeneralization
Some claims are too absolute:
“the root of all evil”
“wreaks havoc in a nation, a family and even an individual life”
“It produces selfishness in even the smallest child”

These are powerful statements, but in academic writing, they should be softened unless you can prove them.

Better alternatives:
“can lead to many forms of wrongdoing”
“often causes serious harm”
“may encourage selfish behavior”

E. Develop examples more specifically
Your examples are broad, but they would be stronger with more detail. For instance, instead of saying a company goes under because of desire, explain how greed can cause corruption, fraud, or exploitation.

Example:
“In business, excessive desire for profit can lead to fraud or unethical decisions, which may damage employees, consumers, and the company itself.”

Style advice for a Chinese-speaking writer

Because Chinese writing sometimes values moral reflection and repetition for emphasis, your essay has a strong reflective style. In English academic argument, however, readers usually prefer:
one clear thesis early,
topic sentences for each paragraph,
fewer repeated ideas,
more specific evidence.

Try to make each paragraph do one job only.

Suggested thesis and conclusion revision

Possible thesis:
“Money is an essential tool in modern life, but when people value it above morality, family, and social responsibility, it can become a cause of serious harm.”

Possible conclusion:
“In conclusion, money itself is not evil; it is a practical necessity. The real danger lies in greed, which can distort human values and lead to harmful actions. Therefore, society should not condemn money itself, but should warn against excessive attachment to it.”

Final priority list for revision
Rewrite the introduction with a clear thesis.
Divide the essay into clear paragraphs.
Correct grammar, capitalization, and word-choice errors.
Replace informal phrases with more academic language.
Add one or two more specific examples.

You have a solid central idea and persuasive potential. With clearer structure and more precise English, this can become a strong argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear argumentative direction: you challenge the common saying “crime does not pay” by showing that crime can benefit some criminals and even institutions. This is a strong central idea, and your essay is thoughtful and interesting. You also use examples effectively to make the discussion concrete. Below are suggestions to help you revise.

Strengths

Clear position
Your main claim is understandable: the phrase is not always true, and may be “wishful thinking.”
The conclusion connects well to the introduction.

Good use of examples
You use examples of violent crime, white-collar crime, and the prison industry.
This gives the essay variety and helps support your argument from different angles.

Logical progression
The essay moves from the common belief, to questioning it, to giving counterexamples.
This structure is appropriate for an argumentative essay.

Main Areas to Improve

A. Make the thesis more explicit
Your argument is present, but the thesis could be sharper in the introduction. Right now, the reader understands your point fully only near the end.

Possible revision idea:
At the end of the first or second paragraph, clearly state:
although the phrase is meant to discourage wrongdoing,
in reality crime sometimes brings financial, social, or institutional rewards,
therefore the saying is misleading.

This will give the essay a stronger focus from the beginning.

B. Strengthen paragraph unity
Some paragraphs contain several ideas at once. Try to make each paragraph focus on one main point.

For example:
Paragraph 2: social function of the phrase
Paragraph 3: crime may still be rational for some offenders
Paragraph 4: famous criminals who still benefited
Paragraph 5: institutions that profit from crime

This will make the essay easier to follow.

C. Develop analysis, not only examples
Your examples are interesting, but sometimes the essay moves too quickly from example to conclusion. After each example, explain more clearly how it proves your point.

For instance:
In the murder example, explain that if punishment is limited and the criminal achieves the intended goal, then the saying “crime does not pay” becomes questionable.
In the prison-industry example, explain more directly that society may publicly condemn crime while economically benefiting from it.

This extra analysis will make your argument more persuasive.

Language and Style

A. Word choice and accuracy
A few words or phrases are awkward or slightly incorrect.

Examples:
“stock broker scams” → better: “stock market scams” or “securities fraud”
“country club prison” is informal; if you want a more academic tone, say “minimum-security prison”
“after, not only saves money” → should be “because it not only saves money” or “which not only saves money”

B. Grammar and sentence form
Most of your sentences are strong, but some are long and could be made clearer.

Example:
“For example, a working class man may recite it…”
  - “working-class” needs a hyphen when used as an adjective: “a working-class man”

“Behavior that is not reinforced in some way will not persist.”
  - This sentence is grammatically correct, but it sounds technical. Make sure this shift in tone is intentional.

C. Tone
Your essay mixes academic and conversational language. Try to keep the tone more consistent.

Examples of more conversational wording:
“country club prison”
“somehow managed”
“play by the rules”

These are vivid, but for a formal argumentative essay, you may want slightly more neutral language.

Evidence and Precision

Some examples may need more precise support:
“the average amount of time that a convicted murderer spends in prison is 15 years”
claims about Michael Milken and prison industry profits

In revision, if this is a formal academic essay, cite sources or make the evidence more careful. Without support, some readers may question the accuracy.

Also, be careful with broad claims such as:
“for most, it is an issue of costs”
This sounds too general unless you provide evidence.

Suggested Revision of the Main Claim

You might revise the thesis to something like this:

“Although the phrase ‘crime does not pay’ is commonly used to promote moral behavior, it oversimplifies reality, because some criminals gain financial or social advantages and some institutions also profit from crime.”

This version is direct, academic, and sets up your body paragraphs well.

Final Advice

For your next draft, focus on:
stating the thesis earlier and more directly,
making each paragraph support one clear sub-point,
adding explanation after each example,
revising awkward wording and maintaining a formal tone,
supporting factual claims more carefully.

Overall, this is a thoughtful essay with a strong central challenge to a familiar belief. With clearer thesis placement, stronger analysis, and more polished language, it can become a very effective argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay presents a clear position: people should be rewarded financially for individual effort. You also try to support this argument with examples from early human life and modern society. The essay is easy to follow overall, and your tone is appropriate for argumentative writing. Below are the main areas to improve in revision.

Strengths

Clear opinion: Your thesis is easy to identify.
Logical overall structure: You move from the past, to the present, to a hypothetical equal society.
Good use of contrast: Comparing earlier times and modern society helps build your argument.
Engaged voice: The essay sounds thoughtful and committed to the topic.

Main issue: make the argument more precise

Your central idea is understandable, but sometimes it is expressed too generally. For example, you often repeat the idea that “the harder you work, the greater the reward,” but you do not always explain why this should be true in modern society.

Try to make the argument more specific:
What kind of work should be rewarded?
Should all hard work be rewarded equally?
What about people who work hard but have fewer opportunities?
Is money the only reward, or just one important reward?

If you address these questions, your essay will sound more mature and persuasive.

Development of ideas

Some paragraphs need more explanation and evidence.

Paragraph 2
You explain survival in the past, but the link to your argument could be sharper. You suggest that effort naturally led to reward, but this was not always because of fairness; sometimes it was simply necessary for survival. Clarify this distinction.

Paragraph 3
This paragraph has a good point about technology and specialized labor, but the explanation is a bit confusing. The sentence about “technical minds” and “paying someone to do their labor” needs clearer wording. Explain more directly how modern work still connects effort and reward.

Paragraph 4
This is an important paragraph, but it becomes too extreme. Statements such as society being “destroyed” are not well supported. Also, equality does not necessarily mean that no one works. Be careful not to create an oversimplified opposite view. A stronger argument would acknowledge that people need basic support, but additional effort can still deserve additional reward.

Organization and coherence

Your essay has a clear beginning, middle, and end, but some transitions can be improved.

For example:
Paragraph 2 focuses on the past.
Paragraph 3 moves to the present.
Paragraph 4 imagines an equal society.

This sequence works, but you should make the connections smoother. Use transition phrases such as:
In contrast
Similarly
However
As a result
Therefore

Also, your conclusion introduces a new question—“is it really about money?”—very late. This is interesting, but it feels underdeveloped. Either discuss this idea earlier in the essay or simplify the conclusion so it reinforces your main claim.

Language and grammar

There are several grammar and word-choice errors. Here are important examples:

“should be financially reward” → “should be financially rewarded”
“took up of majority” → “took up the majority”
“To have clothing, you had to sew” → correct, but “make clothing” may sound more natural
“This is a very simple support of reward equaling effort” → awkward; better: “This simply shows the connection between effort and reward.”
“to held us meet” → “to help us meet”
“on the job and paying someone to do their labor” → unclear; revise for meaning
“treated equal” → “treated equally”
“as advanced of a society” → “as advanced a society”
“jealously” → “jealousy”
“selfish and hoarding” → “selfish and inclined to hoard resources”
“stagnate people” → “stagnant people”

Also watch for comma splices, such as:
“...with little or no effort on our part, it would appear.”
This should be separated into two sentences or rewritten.

Style suggestions

To sound more academic:
Avoid very informal phrases like “Worst case scenario”.
Avoid overgeneralizations like “Life is about rewards.”
Replace emotional or absolute claims with more careful language:
  - “may discourage innovation” instead of “the world would become divided or even destroyed”

Revision priorities

Improve the thesis so it is more precise.
Add clearer reasoning in each body paragraph.
Avoid extreme claims and acknowledge complexity.
Correct grammar and word form errors.
Strengthen the conclusion so it supports, rather than changes, the main argument.

Possible revised thesis

You might write something like:

In modern society, people should be financially rewarded according to their individual effort and contribution, because such rewards encourage responsibility, innovation, and the development of useful skills.

This version is clearer and more academic.

Your essay has a strong foundation. With clearer reasoning, more careful language, and grammatical correction, it can become much more convincing.

Feedback Report

Your essay shows clear engagement with a difficult topic: whether pay can or should reflect a person’s contribution to society. You make an interesting central claim—that contribution is subjective, so equal compensation is impossible—and you support it with examples about inherited wealth, migrant workers, and doctors. Your writing also shows a good range of vocabulary and some sophisticated sentence structures. For revision, focus on clarifying your argument, improving organization, and correcting grammar/word choice.

Strengths
Clear position: Your main idea appears in the introduction and conclusion.
Relevant examples: The contrast between wealthy investors and low-paid workers is effective.
Ambitious language: Phrases such as “preconceived notions,” “contributed nearly nothing,” and “impose on the personal lives” show strong vocabulary.

Main issues with argument and organization

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your current thesis is: “Contribution to society is subjective and therefore impossible to compensate equally.”  
This is a strong start, but the essay sometimes shifts between different questions:
Do rich people deserve their money?
Is democracy fair?
Are some workers underpaid?
Can society measure contribution fairly?

Try to keep one main line of argument. For example:

Possible revised thesis:  
*Although many people believe salaries should reflect social contribution, it is impossible to pay everyone according to contribution because social value is difficult to measure and economic systems reward market demand rather than fairness.*

This will help unify your body paragraphs.

B. Improve paragraph focus
Each body paragraph should have one clear topic sentence and explain how it supports your thesis.

For example:
Paragraph 2: historical example of investors living off wealth
Paragraph 3: inequality between wealthy non-workers and hard laborers
Paragraph 4: belief in equal opportunity is flawed
Paragraph 5: disagreement about who is overpaid proves contribution is subjective

Right now, some paragraphs contain several ideas that are not fully connected. Make the link to your thesis explicit.

C. Develop analysis more fully
Some examples are interesting, but you need to explain them more. For instance, the doctors example could be stronger if you explain:
why some people think doctors are overpaid,
why doctors think their fees are fair,
how this disagreement proves that “contribution” cannot be measured objectively.

Language and grammar corrections

Here are some important sentence-level issues:

“In the 1920s in American” → “In the 1920s in America”
“lived off the interest of their investments” → better: “lived off the returns on their investments”
“live off the interest of investors” → “live off investments” or “live off the interest from investments”
“What about people who work very hard yet they seem to be paid miserably.”  
  → This should be a question: “What about people who work very hard, yet seem to be paid miserably?”
“A migrant work” → “A migrant worker”
“The answer this question” → “The answer to this question”
“Democracry” → “democracy”
“responsibly” → “responsibility”
“much too money” → “too much money”
Quotation marks: “equally’” has incorrect punctuation. Use “equally.” or simply remove quotation marks.

Also watch for article use and awkward phrasing. For example:
“the right of these investors” is grammatical, but “whether these investors had the right” sounds more natural.
“make a fair or moderate salary” → “earn a fair salary”

Style suggestions

A. Avoid overgeneralization
Sentences like “everyone seems to think that doctors make too much money” are too broad. Not everyone thinks this. A better phrase would be:
“many people believe that doctors are overpaid”

B. Use a more formal tone
Some phrases are slightly conversational:
“smart enough or lucky enough” is acceptable, but you might revise it to “fortunate or skilled enough”
“slave away” is vivid but informal; consider “work long hours” or “perform exhausting labor”

Suggested revision plan
Rewrite the thesis so it clearly answers the essay question.
Give each body paragraph one clear purpose.
Add 1–2 sentences of explanation after each example.
Correct grammar, spelling, and word choice errors.
Strengthen the conclusion by restating your main argument more directly.

Example of a revised sentence
Original:  
“Apparently, most people believe that they are underpaid and that others are overpaid.”

Improved:  
“In general, people tend to believe that their own work is undervalued while others receive more money than they deserve.”

Final comment
This essay has a thoughtful central idea and good potential. Your strongest improvement will come from making the argument more focused and ensuring that every paragraph directly supports your thesis. After that, careful editing for grammar and word choice will make your ideas much more persuasive.
