Relevant example: The story about the manager losing his job is concrete and helps readers understand your point.
Logical topic: Work, banking, and daily life are good areas to discuss when arguing that computer literacy is important.

Main Areas to Improve

A. Strengthen the thesis
Your thesis is understandable, but it could be more precise and argumentative. Right now, it mainly says computers are important. Try to explain why computer knowledge is necessary.

For example:
*In modern society, computer literacy is essential because it affects employment, financial security, and people’s ability to adapt to technological change.*

This version gives the reader a roadmap for the body paragraphs.

B. Develop your body paragraphs more fully
The biggest weakness is insufficient development. Each paragraph introduces an idea, but the explanation is too brief.

Paragraph 2
This is your strongest paragraph because it includes an example. However, you should explain more clearly how this example proves your argument. For instance:
Why did the company computerize its inventory?
Why was typing or computer use essential to the manager’s role?
What lesson does this example teach workers in general?

Also, “A vivid illustration of this can be found by examining the work” is awkward. You likely mean the workplace.

Paragraph 3
The banking paragraph is less effective because the connection to your thesis is weak. You mention money transfers, hacking, and checking statements, but you do not explain how these examples show that people need computer knowledge. Make the link explicit:
People now use online banking.
Customers must understand digital systems to protect themselves from fraud.
Basic computer knowledge helps people monitor accounts and use banking services safely.

C. Improve organization and coherence
Your essay has introduction, body, and conclusion, but the structure can be clearer.

A possible structure:
Introduction: importance of computers + thesis
Body paragraph 1: computers and employment
Body paragraph 2: computers and banking/personal finance
Body paragraph 3: need to keep learning as technology changes
Conclusion: restate argument and broader significance

Use stronger transitions:
*First,*  
*In the workplace,*  
*In addition,*  
*Furthermore,*  
*Therefore,*  
*In conclusion,*  

Language and Grammar Issues

There are several spelling, word choice, and sentence-level problems. Below are important corrections:

twenthieth → twentieth
internet working → probably networking or internal operations; if you mean connection through the internet, say the internet systems of large corporations
succesful → successful
examining the work → examining the workplace or looking at the workplace
auto-pasts company → auto-parts company
put all of their inventory on computer → put all of its inventory on a computer system / computerized its inventory
defecit → deficit
Millions of dollars transfer → Millions of dollars are transferred
Because of this hacking has become quite a problem → Because of this, hacking has become a serious problem
to see no errors have been made → to make sure no errors have been made

Grammar note: articles and plurals
As a Chinese speaker, you may want to pay special attention to a/the and singular/plural nouns:
the computer / computers
a computer class
a larger part of society

Style Suggestions
Try to avoid overgeneral or exaggerated expressions unless you can support them:
“every aspect of our lives” is very broad.
You could write:
*computers affect many aspects of modern life.*

Also, vary sentence structure. Many sentences are short and simple. Combining some ideas can improve flow.

Suggested Revision of Your Conclusion
Your final sentence is good in idea but too brief. You could expand it slightly:
*In conclusion, computer literacy is no longer optional. It influences people’s careers, financial safety, and ability to function in a rapidly changing society. For this reason, people must continue learning new computer skills throughout their lives.*

Final Advice
For your next draft:
Write a more specific thesis.
Expand each body paragraph with explanation, not just examples.
Make sure each paragraph clearly supports the thesis.
Correct spelling and grammar carefully.
Add transitions to improve flow.

You have a solid foundation and a relevant argument. With more development and careful editing, this essay can become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: computers have changed life in both positive and negative ways. This is a strong starting point for an argumentative essay because you show a balanced view instead of discussing only one side. Your tone is engaged, and your examples (ATMs, cars, medical technology) help readers understand your ideas. For revision, focus on making your argument more precise, formal, and logically organized.

Main strengths
Clear topic: The essay stays focused on how computers affect people’s lives.
Balanced argument: You discuss both benefits and drawbacks.
Relevant examples: Banking, transportation, and medical technology are good areas to discuss.
Strong personal voice: The reader can see your opinions clearly.

Thesis and argument
Your introduction gives the general topic, but the thesis statement is not sharp enough. You write that computers have changed life “both bad and good,” but this is too broad. A stronger thesis should clearly state your main claim and preview your two sides.

For example:
Stronger thesis: *Although computers have made daily life faster and more convenient, they have also reduced human interaction and increased people’s dependence on machines.*

This version is clearer, more academic, and easier to support in the body paragraphs.

Organization
Your essay has a basic structure:
Introduction
Positive effects
Negative effects
Conclusion

This is good. However, each paragraph can be made more unified.

Paragraph 2
This paragraph is about the benefits of computers, but some sentences are too general, such as:

Instead of broad claims, explain how and why computers improve life. You could group the benefits into categories such as:
communication
efficiency at work
access to services

Paragraph 3
This paragraph has many interesting ideas, but they are crowded together:
job loss
less human interaction
machine failure
overdependence
lack of emotion

These ideas are all relevant, but you should connect them more clearly. You may even divide them into two paragraphs if needed:
one on social effects
one on risks of dependence on technology

Use of evidence and explanation
Your essay would be stronger if you explained your examples more fully. At the moment, some points are stated as opinions without support. For instance:

This is an interesting claim, but it needs explanation. In what situations? Why is this harmful? Can you give a concrete example?

Also, avoid phrases like:
*“It is a common fact…”*
This sounds informal unless you provide evidence.

Try to use a pattern like this:
Point → Example → Explanation
Example:
*Computers increase convenience in banking. For example, ATMs allow people to withdraw money at any time instead of waiting for a bank to open. As a result, computers save time and make financial services more accessible.*

Language and style
Your ideas are understandable, but the essay would benefit from a more formal academic style.

Avoid informal expressions
Examples:
*“I feel”* (used too often)
*“Not to mention…”*
*“EVERYTHING”*
*“a bunch of wires”*

Use more formal alternatives:
*I believe / In my view / It can be argued that*
Avoid all caps in academic writing.

Word choice corrections
Some vocabulary choices are awkward or inaccurate:
*“life styles”* → lifestyles
*“live transpire much quicker”* → life has become much faster
*“proceed with the same transactions”* → perform the same transactions
*“impersonalized”* → made the world more impersonal
*“Interraction”* → interaction
*“obstical”* → obstacle

*“people's life styles”* → people’s lifestyles

*“No longer are the times when people must write a paper manually”*  
  Better: People no longer need to write documents by hand.
*“We now have ATM”* → We now have ATMs
*“machines do fail”* → machines do fail
  (grammatically okay for emphasis, but awkward in tone)
*“incapable, in some instances to function”*  
  Better: incapable, in some instances, of functioning

Also watch for:
comma overuse
unnecessary quotation marks
subject-verb agreement
plural forms

Suggested revision priorities

Correct spelling, grammar, and punctuation errors.

Final comment

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear moral purpose and a sincere voice. You show strong personal engagement with the topic, especially in your belief that cultural understanding can help people live in peace. This gives your writing warmth and conviction. For revision, the main areas to improve are task response, argument development, organization, and language accuracy.

Task response: answer the question more directly
Your essay argues that cultural awareness and unlearning prejudice are very important, but the prompt seems to ask for a discovery or invention of the 20th century. At the moment, your topic feels more like a social change or value than a specific discovery/invention.

How to improve
Explain why this counts as a discovery. For example, you could say it is the “discovery of the value of cultural equality” or “the growing understanding that prejudice harms society.”
Even better, make your thesis more precise:
  - *I argue that the most significant 20th-century development was the growing awareness of cultural equality and the need to overcome prejudice.*
Then support this claim with reasons such as:
  1. It improved human rights.
  2. It gave marginalized groups a voice.
  3. It made societies more peaceful and cooperative.

Organization: make the structure clearer
Your essay has an introduction, body, and conclusion, which is good. However, some sentences are very long and ideas run together. This makes the argument harder to follow.

Suggested structure
Introduction
Introduce the topic
State your clear thesis

Body paragraph 1
Explain the problem of racism/discrimination in the past
Give examples of who was affected

Body paragraph 2
Explain what changed in the 20th century
Show why this change is important for society

Conclusion
Restate your main point
End with a strong final thought

Also, use topic sentences. For example:
*One reason this development is significant is that it challenged long-accepted discrimination.*
*Another reason is that cultural awareness allows societies to learn from diversity.*

Development: use more specific support
Your ideas are thoughtful, but they stay quite general. To make the essay more convincing, add examples.

Possible examples
the Civil Rights Movement in the United States
women’s rights movements
the end of apartheid in South Africa
international human rights organizations
multicultural education and anti-discrimination laws

Specific examples will make your argument stronger and more academic.

Language: grammar, word choice, and spelling
You express meaningful ideas, but there are many language errors that affect clarity. Below are some important corrections.

Examples of corrections
world wide → worldwide
peoples lives → people’s lives
I chose → I choose or I have chosen (depending on context)
discriminitive → discriminatory
more then → more than
excepted → accepted
enought → enough
deeply breed sterotypes → deeply rooted stereotypes
burried → buried
forms are presently being held → forums are presently being held
avalibility → availability
existance → existence

Sentence-level issues
Be careful with comma splices: joining two full sentences with only a comma.

Example:
*The opportunity to learn about the vast world around us is opening up, the availability to study cultural difference...*
  
Better:
*The opportunity to learn about the vast world around us is opening up. The availability of opportunities to study cultural differences and reflect on this knowledge is improving.*

Also, some phrases sound unnatural:
reflect the knowledge on ourselves → reflect on this knowledge ourselves
learning from difference → learning from differences or learning from diversity

Style: make the tone more formal
Your tone is passionate, which is a strength, but some phrases can be made more academic.

For example:
*I greatly value all of humanity* → *I believe that respect for all humanity is essential*
*People have come to realize* → *Society has increasingly recognized*

This will make your essay sound more polished.

A possible revised thesis
You could revise your main idea like this:

*Among the most important developments of the 20th century was the growing awareness of cultural equality and the need to overcome prejudice. This change was significant because it challenged injustice, empowered marginalized groups, and created the possibility of a more peaceful society.*

Final advice
For your next draft, focus on:
making the “discovery/invention” idea clearer,
adding 2–3 specific examples,
dividing long sentences,
correcting spelling and word choice,
using a more formal academic style.

You have a strong ethical message and a clear sense of purpose. With clearer structure and more precise language, this could become a much more powerful argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay raises an interesting critical idea: American society promotes a belief in equal opportunity, but in reality many people are left behind, especially in a fast, technology-driven culture. This is a strong starting point for an argumentative essay. Below are suggestions to help you revise.

Main strengths
Clear critical perspective: You question the ideology of success and equality in the United States.
Relevant topic: Media, technology, individualism, and inequality are important themes.
Ambitious argument: You try to connect national values, modern life, and social failure.

Main problems to revise

A. Clarify your thesis
Right now, your main argument is not fully clear because the first paragraph includes several ideas at once. A stronger thesis should state:
what belief you are criticizing,
why it is misleading, and
what effects it has on society.

For example, your thesis could be revised into something like:

“Although American culture promotes the belief that everyone has an equal opportunity to succeed, modern media and technology reinforce unrealistic standards of success and cause society to ignore structural inequality.”

This is more direct and easier for the reader to follow.

B. Improve organization
Your essay currently has good ideas, but they are packed into long sentences and two dense paragraphs. Try to separate your points more clearly.

A possible structure:
Introduction: Explain the ideology of success and present your thesis.
Body paragraph 1: Show how media promotes certain models of success.
Body paragraph 2: Explain how technology and fast-paced life increase individualism.
Body paragraph 3: Discuss how inequality is blamed on individuals instead of social conditions.
Conclusion: Restate your argument and explain why it matters.

C. Develop your evidence
Your essay makes broad claims, but it needs more support. For example:
What kinds of TV commercials or programs promote these ideas?
How does technology change people’s relationships?
Why is equal opportunity not really equal?

You do not need many sources in this draft, but you need specific examples. Even one or two concrete examples would make the argument much stronger.

Sentence-level issues

A. Run-on sentences
Several sentences are too long and contain too many ideas. This makes the writing difficult to understand. Break them into shorter sentences.

For example, this sentence:

> “In the 20th cen, the values of/that the United States was founded on have changed in meaning and sense when applied to modern day America, nothing was ever equal.”

could become:

“In the twentieth century, the values on which the United States was founded changed in meaning when applied to modern America. In reality, equality was never fully achieved.”

B. Word choice
Some phrases are awkward or unclear:
“one nolonger sees the realitive humanness” → “people no longer recognize their shared humanity”
“attribute thier inadequacy to achieve” → “attribute their failure to succeed”
“The parts of the new machine that need oiled and regulated” → this is an interesting image, but it is incomplete. You need to explain it fully.

C. Tone and formality
Avoid symbols like “&” in academic writing. Write “and.”
Also capitalize correctly:
Americans
TV
twentieth century (usually written out in formal writing)

Grammar and mechanics to correct
Please carefully proofread for spelling, punctuation, and grammar. Some common errors in this draft:

comercials → commercials
everyday → every day
substitued → substituted
nolonger → no longer
realitive → relative
unsuccessfull → unsuccessful
thier → their
oiled should be in a fuller phrase, such as “need to be oiled and regulated”

Also check:
comma splices
subject-verb agreement
article use (“the,” “a,” “an”)
parallel structure

For example:
> “People are more mobile, can work more, and buy more things, but time for relaxation and family are often substitued with T.V.”

Better:
“People are more mobile, work longer hours, and buy more things, but time for relaxation and family is often replaced by TV.”

Content advice for revision
Your most promising idea is that society blames individuals for failure while ignoring larger systems. This is a sophisticated argument. To strengthen it, explain:
What are the “written and unwritten rules”?
Who creates these standards?
Why do some people have fewer opportunities?
How do media and technology hide these inequalities?

If you answer these questions, your essay will become much more persuasive.

Final suggestion
When revising, focus first on big issues:
thesis
organization
examples

Then edit for grammar and style. Do not try to fix everything at once.

You have strong ideas, but they need clearer structure and more precise language. With revision, this could become a thoughtful and compelling argument.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a strong personal voice and a clear concern about the social effects of technology. You also use specific experiences—travel, your grandmother’s cancer treatment, schooling in the U.S., and learning Spanish—which makes the essay feel sincere and engaging. The main area to improve is argument focus: your introduction discusses technology in general, but the body shifts to education, cultural awareness, and U.S. attitudes. To make the essay more persuasive, you need a clearer thesis and stronger connections between ideas.

Clarify your main argument
Right now, your essay seems to argue that technology can isolate people and reduce cultural awareness, especially among young people in the U.S. If that is your real position, state it directly in the introduction.

For example:
Possible thesis: “Although technology has brought enormous benefits, it has also contributed to isolation, weak global awareness, and limited language learning among young people in the United States.”

This would help readers understand what you will prove in the body paragraphs.

Improve organization
The essay currently moves from:
technology in general,
personal benefits of science and technology,
youth in the U.S.,
geography and language education.

These ideas are interesting, but the links between them are not always clear. Try this structure:

Introduction: technology has both benefits and harms; thesis about isolation and cultural ignorance.
Body paragraph 1: benefits of technology.
Body paragraph 2: technology can encourage isolation/self-centeredness.
Body paragraph 3: effects on education: geography, foreign languages, and global awareness.
Conclusion: restate argument and suggest what society should do.

Each paragraph should begin with a topic sentence that clearly states the paragraph’s main point.

Support your claims more carefully
Some claims are too broad, for example:
“a great majority of Americans live in their own personal closets”
“Geography classes rarely, if ever, exist”
“Our awareness of other people… is very low”

These statements may sound exaggerated unless you provide evidence. In an argumentative essay, try to balance personal experience with broader support. You could say:
“In my experience, many students seemed to know little about other countries.”
“Some schools place limited emphasis on geography and foreign language study.”

This sounds more precise and academic.

Work on coherence and logic
Sometimes the connection between technology and your examples is not fully explained. For instance, the paragraph about geography classes is really about education, not directly about technology. You need to explain how technology contributes to this problem. Does it make students less curious? Does it create a false sense of connection to the world? Add sentences that explain the cause-and-effect relationship.

Sentence-level language issues
You have some strong vocabulary, but sometimes the style becomes too dramatic or unnatural for an academic essay.

Examples:
“This thing, technology…” → awkward. Better: “Technology…”
“the greatest Nemesis of our world” → “Nemesis” is too dramatic and should not be capitalized. Better: “one of the greatest threats to our world”
“welded with certain aspects of and certain people in our societies” → unclear. Better: “when misused by people or institutions”

Also, watch for run-on sentences and punctuation. For example:

Original:
“Science and technology have allowed me to travel to this part of the world, they have provided a living for my family…”

This should be separated or joined correctly:
“Science and technology have allowed me to travel to this part of the world. They have provided a living for my family…”

Grammar and phrasing corrections
A few specific points:

“has affected societies” → better: “have affected societies” because the subject is plural: “development and implementation.”
“a large detriment to our youth” → better: “highly detrimental to young people”
“I would concur” → formal but slightly stiff; “I agree” is simpler.
“that I mentioned before” → unnecessary; delete.
“The effort of the United States has caused our minds to be heard” → unclear meaning. You likely need to rewrite this sentence completely.

Strengthen the conclusion
Your final sentence is difficult to understand and does not clearly conclude the essay. A conclusion should:
restate your main argument,
summarize the main points,
leave the reader with a final thought.

For example:
“In conclusion, technology itself is not the problem; the problem is how society uses it. If education continues to neglect geography, languages, and cultural understanding, technology may connect our devices while leaving our minds isolated.”

Final advice
For revision, focus on:
writing a clear thesis,
improving organization,
making the connection between technology and education/cultural awareness more explicit,
revising grammar and sentence structure for clarity.

You have meaningful ideas and vivid personal examples. With clearer structure and more precise language, this could become a strong argumentative essay.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0007.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a consistent position: computers are important in modern life and people have become dependent on them. The structure is also easy to follow, with an introduction, body paragraphs, and a conclusion. This is a good foundation. To improve the essay, focus on thesis clarity, paragraph development, grammar, word choice, and repetition.

Strengths
Clear subject: The essay stays focused on computers and their influence on daily life.
Logical organization: You move from general importance, to examples of use, to dependence and effects.
Appropriate tone: The essay sounds formal enough for an academic paragraph/essay.

Main areas to revise

A. Make the thesis more specific
Your introduction presents the topic well, but the main argument is still broad. A stronger thesis should clearly state how computers changed people’s lives.

Current idea:
> Computers have significantly changed people's lives.

Stronger version:
> Computers have transformed modern life by increasing efficiency at home, in schools, and in businesses, but this convenience has also made people highly dependent on them.

This thesis is better because it gives the essay a clear direction.

B. Develop each body paragraph with clearer topic sentences and examples
Some paragraphs repeat the same point: computers are everywhere and useful. Try to give each paragraph one main idea.

For example:
Paragraph 1: Computers are now part of daily life in many places.
Paragraph 2: Computers save time and improve efficiency in schools and businesses.
Paragraph 3: People’s dependence on computers creates problems when computers fail.

Also, add more specific examples. For instance:
In homes: paying bills online, communicating, entertainment
In schools: research, online learning, writing assignments
In businesses: storing data, managing accounts, communication

C. Avoid repetition
You repeat similar ideas and phrases, such as:
“part of everyday life”
“people rely on computers”
“computers speed up tasks”

Repetition makes the essay sound less developed. Try using synonyms or combining ideas more effectively.

For example:
“Computers have become essential in daily routines.”
“Many people depend on digital technology for work and study.”
“They reduce the time needed to complete complex tasks.”

D. Improve grammar and word choice
There are several grammar and vocabulary problems. Here are some important corrections:

“In people's homes calculators as usually found”  
   → “In people’s homes, calculators are usually found”  
   (“as” should be “are”)

“speed-up”  
   → “speed up”  
   (verb form should not have a hyphen)

“laboreous”  
   → “laborious” or “time-consuming”  
   (“laboreous” is not the correct word)

“Schools use computers to teach children because it is fun and exciting.”  
   → “Schools use computers to teach children because they make learning more engaging.”  
   (“it” is unclear; “they” refers to computers)

“since the invention of the calculator (the first computer)”  
   → This idea is inaccurate. A calculator is not the first computer.  
   You could write:  
   “People’s lives have changed significantly since the invention of the computer.”

“to help everyday life run smoothly”  
   → “to help daily life run smoothly”  
   This sounds more natural.

E. Be careful with factual accuracy
The phrase “the calculator (the first computer)” is incorrect. In academic writing, factual accuracy is important. If you are unsure, use a safer statement.

F. Strengthen the conclusion
Your conclusion repeats the introduction, but it can be more effective if it briefly summarizes your main points and ends with a strong final thought.

Possible revision:
> In conclusion, computers have become essential in homes, schools, and workplaces because they save time and improve efficiency. However, this convenience has also made people dependent on technology. As computers continue to shape modern life, people should appreciate their benefits while also recognizing the risks of relying on them too much.

Sentence-level advice
Try to avoid switching between “people” and “one”:
> “At the office, in school, or while shopping one will find...”

This is grammatically possible, but it sounds inconsistent. Better:
> “At the office, at school, or while shopping, people can see computers being used everywhere.”

Also, watch punctuation and spacing. There are extra spaces in some places.

Overall revision goal
For your next draft, try to:
Write a more specific thesis.
Give each body paragraph one clear focus.
Add concrete examples.
Correct grammar and vocabulary errors.
Reduce repetition.

Final comment
This essay shows that you have a solid understanding of the topic and can organize your ideas clearly. The next step is to make your argument more precise and your language more natural and accurate. With revision, this can become a much stronger academic essay.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0008.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has an ambitious and interesting idea: you connect Einstein’s theory of relativity to changes in human thought, time, and global politics. This is a strong topic for an argumentative essay because it moves beyond science and asks how ideas shape society. You also show a personal voice, which gives the essay energy. To revise successfully, focus on clarity, accuracy, organization, and grammar.

Main strengths
Original perspective: You do not simply describe relativity; you argue that its deeper impact on human thinking has not yet fully happened.
Big-picture thinking: You connect science, philosophy, and politics in an intellectually ambitious way.
Clear interest in the topic: Your engagement with the subject makes the essay more compelling.

Main problems to revise

A. Clarify your thesis
At the moment, your central argument is interesting but not precise enough. I think your main claim is something like:

“Einstein’s theory of relativity changed humanity’s understanding of time and knowledge, but society has not fully absorbed its philosophical meaning and has instead mainly used Einstein’s ideas for destructive purposes such as atomic weapons.”

If this is your argument, state it clearly at the end of the introduction.

B. Improve organization
Some paragraphs move too quickly from one idea to another. For example, you shift from relativity, to Socrates, to time, to atomic bombs, to the Cold War. These connections may make sense to you, but the reader needs clearer transitions.

A possible structure:
Introduction: Introduce Einstein and present your thesis.
Paragraph 1: Explain how relativity challenges traditional ideas of absolute knowledge and time.
Paragraph 2: Explain how this new understanding could affect human thinking and philosophy.
Paragraph 3: Discuss how Einstein’s scientific work became connected to atomic weapons and global fear.
Conclusion: Reaffirm that humanity has not yet fully understood the philosophical meaning of relativity.

C. Use more accurate content
Some claims are historically or scientifically unclear:
“his and his wife’s theory” — Einstein’s wife was not generally recognized as co-author of relativity in standard academic writing. Unless you provide evidence, avoid this claim.
“With this theory also came the area of quantum mechanics.” Relativity and quantum mechanics are different fields. This sentence is inaccurate.
“The Socratic idea that all was known” — this is not a clear or accurate description of Socrates. Be careful with philosophical references.
Atomic bombs: Einstein did not invent the atomic bomb. You may say his work contributed indirectly to later scientific developments, but the relationship should be expressed carefully.

In argumentative writing, strong ideas become stronger when the facts are accurate.

Language issues

A. Spelling and word form
There are several spelling errors:
histories → history’s
Einstien → Einstein
socratic → Socratic
nostolgic → nostalgic
utopic is possible, but utopian is more common
short comings → shortcomings
Hawkings → Hawking

B. Sentence structure
Some sentences are fragments or are not grammatically complete:
“The socratic idea that all was known.”  
  This is a fragment. It should be connected to the previous sentence.
“USA-USSR; with Japan and Germany as the ‘winners’.”  
  This is unclear and incomplete.

Try to write more complete, direct sentences.

C. Punctuation and capitalization
Do not capitalize words in the middle of a sentence unless they are proper nouns.
Avoid semicolons unless both parts are complete sentences.
Example:  
  “The point is my notion of time has changed; And I think…”  
  Better: “The point is that my notion of time has changed, and I think…”

Style suggestions
Try to reduce vague or overly poetic phrases unless you explain them. For example:
“The philosopher’s stone was pulverized into utopic and nostalgic dust.”
This sounds powerful, but it is not clear. In academic writing, clarity is more important than dramatic wording.

Also, define key terms:
What do you mean by “closure”?
What do you mean by “earthtime”?
How exactly does relativity change “humanity’s philosophy”?

Example of a clearer revised sentence
Original:  
“For now we are left with a world that has used Einstien’s ‘invention’ for intentions not worthy of the genius; namely: atomic bombs.”

Revised:  
“So far, however, humanity has applied Einstein’s scientific ideas more visibly to destructive technologies, especially atomic weapons, than to a deeper rethinking of human knowledge and time.”

Final advice
For revision, focus on these priorities:
Write a clear thesis.
Organize each paragraph around one main idea.
Check factual accuracy.
Correct spelling, punctuation, and sentence fragments.
Prefer clear explanation over abstract language.

You have the basis of a thoughtful essay. With clearer structure and more precise language, your argument could become much more persuasive.

Strengths

Current idea:
> shopping malls are more of a detriment than a blessing.

Stronger version:
> In Kalamazoo, shopping malls have damaged downtown life by drawing businesses and wealthy residents away from the city center, reducing support for poorer neighborhoods, and weakening the sense of community once found downtown.

This helps guide the whole essay.

B. Improve organization
Right now, some paragraphs contain many ideas at once. Try this structure:

Introduction  
   Background + clear thesis

Body paragraph 1: Historical development of downtown  
   Explain how Kalamazoo used to function as a central, shared space.

Body paragraph 2: How malls and suburban growth changed shopping patterns  
   Explain movement of businesses and wealthy residents to suburbs.

Body paragraph 3: Effects on poor and working-class residents  
   Focus on inequality, transportation, neighborhood decline.

Body paragraph 4: Counterpoint or exception  
   Mention colleges and festivals as efforts to revive downtown, but explain why they are not enough.

Conclusion  
   Restate argument and explain why it matters.

C. Use stronger evidence and explanation
Your essay makes good claims, but some points need more support. For example:
Why exactly do malls hurt downtown businesses?
How does lack of transportation affect poor residents?
Why are festivals and colleges not enough to restore downtown?

If this is a personal/opinion essay, you can still strengthen it with examples:
empty storefronts downtown
people preferring parking and convenience at malls
fewer local services in poorer neighborhoods

Language and grammar issues to revise

A. Word choice
Several words are inaccurate or awkward:
“clientel” → clientele
“the flu from the inner cities” → probably “the flight from the inner cities”
“means of transportation” is understandable, but “transportation routes” may fit better
“more poorer people” → “poorer people”
“larger factories and office building” → “larger factories and office buildings”

B. Pronouns and determiners
“where the could keep a distance” → “where they could keep their distance”
“There neighborhoods” → “Their neighborhoods”
“many business try” → “many businesses try”

C. Sentence structure and punctuation
Some sentences are too long or run together. Break them into shorter sentences. For example:

Original:
> Businesses that could move out of the downtown district did, and still do, and relocate in strip malls near the main malls or in the malls themselves.

Revision:
> Businesses that could leave the downtown district did so, and many still continue to relocate. They moved to strip malls near major shopping centers or into the malls themselves.

Also revise capitalization:
“and still could be!” should not stand alone as a fragment. Attach it to the previous sentence or rewrite it fully.

D. Formal academic tone
Avoid informal expressions like:
“Why should we…”
“nice indoor mall that has everything”

Instead, write:
> Many consumers prefer malls because they offer convenience, parking, and a wide variety of stores in one location.

Content suggestions
A few ideas need clearer logic:
The essay begins by saying the invention of the shopping mall changed Kalamazoo. More accurately, you mean the development or spread of shopping malls.
Be careful not to oversimplify social groups. For example, “the rich” and “the poor” can sound too general. Try:
  - wealthier residents
  - low-income residents
  - working-class families

Example of a revised sentence
Original:
> Everyone was near the central city - so everyone could use the central city.

Revision:
> Because most residents lived near the city center, downtown was accessible to people from different social classes.

Final advice
For your next draft, focus first on big issues:
Write a more detailed thesis.
Reorganize body paragraphs by topic.
Add clearer explanation for each claim.

Then edit for grammar and style:
Correct word choice and spelling.
Break long sentences into clearer ones.
Use a more formal academic tone.

You have a thoughtful argument and a strong central concern about inequality. With clearer structure and more careful editing, this essay can become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: television has great potential, but in reality it has mostly made society passive, less thoughtful, and less connected to family life. This is a strong argumentative idea, and your essay stays focused on it. You also use an effective contrast between the positive possibilities of television and its negative social influence. That contrast gives your argument a clear structure.

What you do well

Clear thesis and stance  
   Your main argument is understandable from the introduction and continues throughout the essay.

Logical organization  
   The essay moves in a sensible way:
   - introduction to television’s importance
   - possible benefits
   - actual negative effects
   - conclusion  
   This is a good argumentative structure.

Appropriate academic tone  
   Your word choice is often formal and suitable for an essay, such as “educational programming,” “current events,” and “influenced society.”

Main areas to revise

Strengthen the thesis
Your thesis is interesting, but it can be made more precise. Right now, the essay says television changed society and became a way to “waste time.” Later you argue more specifically that it reduces family interaction and encourages laziness. Put these key points directly into the thesis.

Possible revision:  
“Although television has the potential to educate people and broaden their understanding of the world, it has mainly influenced society negatively by encouraging escapism, weakening family interaction, and promoting passivity.”

This will help the reader know exactly what your body paragraphs will prove.

Develop body paragraphs with clearer support
Your ideas are good, but some claims are too general. For example:
“T.V. is a major cause of lack of togetherness”
“has also resulted in an increase in laziness”

These are strong claims, so they need more explanation or examples. Try to explain how television causes these effects. You could mention:
family members watching separately instead of talking
people spending leisure time passively instead of reading, exercising, or participating in community life
entertainment programs distracting people from social issues

Each body paragraph should have:
a topic sentence
explanation
example
link back to your thesis

Avoid overgeneralization
Phrases like “most Americans” and “millions of Americans” sound too broad unless you provide evidence. In academic writing, it is better to be more cautious.

Instead of:
“In the minds of most Americans...”
Try:
“For many Americans...”
“In many households...”
“Television is often viewed as...”

This makes your argument sound more balanced and credible.

Language and grammar corrections

Here are some important sentence-level revisions:

“has potential” → “has the potential”
“far away places” → “faraway places” or “distant places”
“than cannot be given by newspaper” → “than can be given by newspapers”
“The T.V.” / “TV” / “television”  
  Be consistent. In formal writing, “television” is best.
“watching sit-coms, soap-operas” → “watching sitcoms and soap operas”
“a major cause of lack of togetherness found in many American families”  
  Better: “a major cause of the lack of togetherness in many American families”
“The more time one spends watching TV, the less motivated he becomes”  
  Better and more modern: “The more time people spend watching television, the less motivated they may become.”
“00 years ago” → “100 years ago”
“integrety” → “integrity”
“dificulties” → “difficulties”
“has becomed” → “has become”

Improve sentence style
Some sentences are too long and contain too many ideas. Breaking them into shorter sentences will improve clarity.

For example:  
“Television has potential to offer wonderful things to its viewers...”  
This sentence lists many ideas at once. You could divide it into two sentences: one about education and culture, another about news and artistic expression.

Also watch article use, which is a common challenge for Chinese speakers:
“by newspaper” → “by a newspaper” or better “by newspapers”
“the invention of television” is correct because you refer to a specific invention
“has become the escape route of the masses” sounds unnatural; “an escape for the masses” is smoother

Suggested revision priorities

Rewrite the thesis to include your main negative effects.
Organize body paragraphs so each one focuses on one effect:
   - escapism
   - family disconnection
   - laziness/passivity
Add examples or explanations for each claim.
Edit grammar, spelling, and article use.
Use more cautious academic language instead of absolute statements.

Final comment

This essay shows strong critical thinking and a clear argumentative direction. Your main task now is to make the argument more precise, better supported, and more polished grammatically. You already have a solid foundation; with clearer evidence and careful editing, this can become a convincing academic essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that the polio vaccine was one of the most important inventions of the twentieth century because it reduced suffering and helped later medical progress. This is a strong topic, and your writing shows genuine engagement with the issue. You also try to explain both the social effect and the scientific significance, which is a good argumentative strategy.

What is working well

Clear central claim  
   Your thesis is easy to identify: the polio vaccine was great because it changed population patterns and created a foundation for future medical advances.

Strong sense of importance  
   You communicate urgency and emotional weight effectively. Phrases about children being saved from death or disability help readers understand why the topic matters.

Attempt to connect science and society  
   You do not only describe the vaccine medically; you also discuss its wider effect on families, children, and historical change. That makes the essay more interesting.

Main areas to revise

Make the argument more precise
Some claims are too broad or unsupported. For example:
“Perhaps the greatest invention and discovery is the polio vaccine”
“The effects of such a vaccine are countless”
“The polio vaccine stopped death in its path”
“Every few years a new one comes along to normalize an overpopulated world”

These statements sound dramatic, but in academic argument they need evidence or more careful wording. Try to avoid overgeneralization.

Better approach:  
“The polio vaccine was one of the most significant medical developments of the twentieth century because it reduced child mortality and disability and encouraged confidence in preventive medicine.”

Improve organization
Your essay has an introduction, body, and conclusion, but the body paragraph mixes several ideas:
fear of polio
childhood vaccination
the Baby Boom
disease as population control

These ideas need clearer structure. Consider this organization:

Introduction: background + thesis  
Body Paragraph 1: how polio affected people before the vaccine  
Body Paragraph 2: how the vaccine reduced death/disability and improved childhood health  
Body Paragraph 3: how the success of vaccination encouraged later medical advances  
Conclusion: restate argument and significance

Use a more formal academic tone
Some expressions are too conversational:
“For sure”
“I’m sure”
“for each individual to decide”
“Things weren’t impossible any more”

Also, using “&” is informal in academic writing; write and instead.

Be careful with historical logic
Your discussion of the Baby Boom is interesting, but it is not fully convincing. You say the vaccine may have contributed to a larger surviving group, but then you say whether it had anything to do with the Baby Boom is “for each individual to decide.” In an argumentative essay, you should take a clear position and support it.

If you are not certain, use cautious language:
“The vaccine may have contributed to higher child survival rates during this period.”
“Although it did not directly cause the Baby Boom, it likely improved the survival of children born during those years.”

Grammar and word choice
There are several language errors that interfere with clarity.

Specific corrections
“For centuries before” → “For centuries”
“mamed” → “maimed”
“starting at the middle of the century” → “beginning in the middle of the twentieth century”
“future’s children” → “future generations of children”
“20 year olds” → “twenty-year-olds”
“as a child” → “as children”
“there was an effect on the population as a whole” → “the vaccine affected the population as a whole”
“than time” → “that time”
“The vaccination did not CAUSE” → avoid all caps; use italics only if necessary, or simply write “did not directly cause”
“dead virus” → more accurately, “inactive” or “weakened” virus, depending on the vaccine type

Sentence control and punctuation
Some sentences are too long and contain comma splices. For example:

“Unfortunately this still occurs, but with increasing medical & technological advances starting at the middle of the century many people have been rescued from the torment of disease.”

This would be clearer if divided:
“Unfortunately, this still occurs. However, because of medical and technological advances beginning in the middle of the twentieth century, many people have been saved from the effects of disease.”

Suggested revised thesis
You could use something like this:

“The polio vaccine was one of the most important medical achievements of the twentieth century because it protected children from death and disability and increased confidence in modern vaccination, paving the way for later public health advances.”

Final advice
For revision, focus on three priorities:
make your claims more specific and supported,
organize each paragraph around one main idea,
edit carefully for grammar, word choice, and formal style.

You have a good foundation and a meaningful topic. With clearer structure and more precise language, your argument will become much stronger.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: the airplane has greatly changed human life. You also organize the body by major areas of impact—travel, business, entertainment, and international relations—which gives the essay a logical structure. This is a strong foundation. For revision, focus on three main areas: thesis and argument development, grammar and word choice, and academic style.

Strengths
Clear topic: You consistently argue that airplanes changed people’s lives.
Good organization: Each body paragraph focuses on one area.
Relevant examples: Travel time, international business, entertainers, and war all support your main idea.

Main Revision Priorities

A. Make the thesis more precise
Your introduction gives the general idea, but the thesis could be more direct and academic. Right now, the first paragraph repeats “drastically changed” several times and includes informal symbols like “&”.

A stronger thesis should:
clearly state your claim;
preview the main points.

For example:
“The invention of the airplane has transformed modern life by making travel faster, expanding international business, increasing the mobility of entertainment industries, and reshaping relations between nations.”

This version is concise and previews the body paragraphs.

B. Develop analysis, not just examples
Some paragraphs list effects but do not explain them deeply enough. In argumentative writing, examples should be followed by analysis.

For instance, in the business paragraph, instead of only saying countries can work together more easily, explain why this matters:
faster face-to-face meetings build trust;
companies can manage global branches;
goods and people move more efficiently.

Similarly, in the entertainment paragraph, the Michael Jackson example is too specific and informal. It would be better to make the point more generally:
“Air travel allows performers and athletes to tour internationally, which helps them reach larger audiences and participate in more events.”

C. Improve the conclusion
Your final sentence is unclear:  
“The invention of the airplane has had many positive effects-, unfortunately, however, it was brought about some negative changes, also.”

Problems:
punctuation is incorrect;
“it was brought about” is ungrammatical;
“unfortunately, however” is repetitive.

A clearer conclusion would be:
“Overall, although the airplane has brought some negative consequences, its influence on travel, business, entertainment, and international relations has been overwhelmingly significant.”

Language Issues to Correct

A. Word form and grammar
Please revise the following:

“has been drastically effected” → “has been drastically affected”  
  (“effect” is usually a noun; “affect” is the verb)

“have been significantly shorten” → “have been significantly shortened”

“choosen destination” → “chosen destination”

“previously possibly” → “previously possible”

“musicans” → “musicians”

“the do” → “they do”

“in their perspective countries” → “in their respective countries”

“Pearl Habor” → “Pearl Harbor”

B. Sentence structure
Some sentences are awkward or run too long. For example:

Original:  
“Air transportation allows people to travel from place to place more quickly & efficeintly & overseas travel has been drastically effected.”

Better:  
“Air transportation allows people to travel more quickly and efficiently, and it has greatly expanded overseas travel.”

Try to avoid overloading one sentence with too many ideas.

C. Formal academic style
Avoid:
“&” → use “and”
contractions like “I’m,” “would’ve”
conversational phrases like “As I’ve already said” and “I’m going to touch on”

Use more formal alternatives:
“As noted earlier”
“The final area to consider is…”

Also, first person (“in my opinion,” “I have seen this”) is not always wrong, but in this essay it is stronger to focus on evidence rather than personal opinion.

Organization Tips
Your paragraph structure is generally good, but each body paragraph will be stronger if you follow this pattern:

Topic sentence
Explanation
Example
Analysis
Link back to thesis

For example, in the travel paragraph, after comparing Michigan and California, explain how shorter travel time changes tourism, family visits, and access to distant places.

Final Advice
For your next draft:
Rewrite the thesis more clearly.
Replace informal language with academic wording.
Correct grammar and spelling carefully.
Add more explanation after each example.
Strengthen the conclusion by summarizing your argument.

You already have a solid idea and clear structure. With more precise language and deeper analysis, this essay can become much stronger.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear main idea: AIDS changed not only people’s health concerns but also social attitudes and behavior. You also use a strong example, Ryan White, to show how fear and prejudice affected real people. This gives your argument emotional force. To improve the essay, focus on accuracy, organization, grammar, and academic tone.

Strengths
Clear topic: You consistently discuss how AIDS changed people’s lives and thinking.
Relevant example: The Ryan White example supports your point about fear and discrimination.
Cause-and-effect argument: You explain that AIDS led to paranoia, stigma, and changes in sexual behavior.

Main areas to revise

A. Improve your thesis
Your introduction gives background, but the main argument is not sharp enough. A stronger thesis should clearly state your main points.

Current idea: AIDS changed people’s lives and ways of thinking.  
Better thesis: *The AIDS epidemic significantly changed society by increasing fear and prejudice, challenging attitudes toward sexuality, and encouraging people to think more carefully about health and relationships.*

This tells the reader exactly what your essay will discuss.

B. Use more accurate and careful language
Some statements are too absolute or factually weak. In academic writing, avoid overgeneralization.

For example:
“it always leads to death” → This is too absolute and is no longer accurate in general. Better: *it was widely seen as a fatal disease, especially in the early years of the epidemic.*
“rarely occurs” about casual sex → This is too broad and difficult to prove. Better: *many people became more cautious about casual sex.*

Also, “AIDS” and “HIV” are not exactly the same. If possible, be careful with terms. If your class expects precision, you may want to mention HIV as the virus and AIDS as the disease caused by advanced HIV infection.

C. Organize body paragraphs more clearly
Each body paragraph should have one main idea.

A possible structure:
Introduction: background + thesis
Paragraph 1: fear and prejudice toward people with AIDS
Paragraph 2: Ryan White as evidence of public panic and discrimination
Paragraph 3: changes in sexual behavior and attitudes toward relationships
Conclusion: restate argument and reflect on broader social change

Right now, some ideas are mixed together, especially fear, medical uncertainty, and changes in relationships.

D. Make the tone more academic
Avoid phrases that sound too conversational:
“I feel this has really made people think” → Better: *This epidemic forced many people to reconsider their behavior.*
“I’m sure more changes will come about” → Better: *As public education increases, further social changes may occur.*

Academic writing usually sounds more objective and formal.

Grammar and language corrections

Here are some important sentence-level issues:

“many peoples lives” → *many people’s lives*
“awhile” → *a while*
“it’s presence” → *its presence*  
  (“it’s” = “it is”; “its” = possessive)
“untill” → *until*
“it’s consequences” → *its consequences*
“devistating” → *devastating*
“definately” → *definitely*
“there is more of stigma” → *there is more of a stigma*
“With new discoveries of AIDS” → *With new discoveries about AIDS*
“at an all time high” → *at an all-time high*
“some undetectable strands of AIDS do exist” → This wording is unclear. Better: *Doctors have found that some people may carry the virus without obvious symptoms.*
“one night stands” → *one-night stands*

Also watch sentence boundaries. For example:
“Also, when Ryan White, a boy who contracted AIDS through a blood transfusion was diagnosed…”  
Add commas: *Also, when Ryan White, a boy who contracted AIDS through a blood transfusion, was diagnosed…*

Example of a revised sentence
Original:  
*It has caused a lot of prejudice and paranoia among people which has torn them apart.*

Revised:  
*The epidemic created widespread fear and prejudice, which divided communities and led many people to treat patients unfairly.*

This version is more precise and formal.

Final advice
For revision, focus on these priorities:
Write a clearer thesis.
Separate each body paragraph by one main point.
Replace broad claims with more careful, supportable statements.
Correct spelling, possessives, and article use.
Use a more formal academic tone.

You already have a meaningful topic and a strong central concern about social discrimination. With clearer structure and more precise language, your essay will become much more persuasive.

Problems:

the commas interrupt the sentence awkwardly

“testimonial landmark to 9 presidential administrations” is very unclear. Try a simpler sentence.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0015.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a logical general progression: you begin with the discovery of penicillin, then discuss the development of synthetic antibiotics, and end with the broader impact on medicine and industry. Your vocabulary is also fairly strong, especially in scientific expressions such as “naturally-occurring antibiotics,” “biologically active,” and “synthesis routes.” This gives the essay an appropriately academic tone.

Main issue: argument and focus
At the moment, the essay reads more like a short historical summary than an argumentative essay. An argumentative essay usually needs:
a clear thesis that makes a debatable claim,
supporting points that clearly defend that claim,
and, sometimes, consideration of alternative views.

Your current thesis seems to be that penicillin had an enormous influence on medicine, science, and industry. This is a good idea, but it should be stated more directly and specifically in the introduction.

For example, you could revise your thesis to something like:
“The discovery of penicillin transformed modern society because it revolutionized medical treatment, stimulated pharmaceutical research, and created a powerful global drug industry.”

Then each body paragraph can focus on one of these three effects.

Organization
Your essay has three paragraphs, but the second paragraph is doing too much work. It mixes:
decline in discovery of new antibiotics,
chemical synthesis,
growth of companies,
easier access to antibiotics,
and university research.

Try to organize by one main idea per paragraph:
Medical importance of penicillin
Scientific and chemical development after penicillin
Industrial and social impact

This will make the structure easier for readers to follow.

Use of evidence and precision
Your ideas are interesting, but many statements are very broad. In academic argument, readers need specific evidence or examples.

For instance:
“one of the greatest advances” → compared to what? Why?
“many uses” → what kinds of infections?
“huge enterprises, such as Upjohn” → how did they grow? Why is this important?
“thousands of universities and companies” → this sounds exaggerated unless supported.

Also, one sentence is scientifically inaccurate:
“It was now much easier to obtain antibiotics for simple illnesses, such as the common cold.”
The common cold is caused by viruses, so antibiotics do not treat it. You may mean that antibiotics became widely used, and sometimes overused, for minor illnesses. If so, say that clearly.

Style: avoid too much passive voice
You often use passive constructions:
“was discovered”
“was attempted”
“were synthesized”
“were found”

Passive voice is common in science writing, but too much of it makes your prose repetitive and less direct. Try mixing in active voice:
“Scientists discovered penicillin…”
“Chemists attempted to increase production…”
“Researchers synthesized older drugs efficiently…”

Grammar and word choice
Here are some important language corrections:

“As discoveries of new antibiotics decreased”  
  Better: “As the discovery of new antibiotics slowed”

“It was also attemped”  
  Spelling: attempted

“through synthesis”  
  Better: “by synthetic methods” or “through chemical synthesis”

“caused a need for more research, more companies, and more medical chemists”  
  Acceptable, but more natural: “created demand for further research, pharmaceutical companies, and trained medicinal chemists”

“The discovery of one compound led to the increase of health of millions of people”  
  Better: “The discovery of this compound improved the health of millions of people”

“research of their uses”  
  Better: “research on their uses”

“a small bacteria”  
  Incorrect form. Bacteria is plural; singular is bacterium.  
  Also, penicillin is produced by a fungus, not a bacterium. So revise to:  
  “the work begun by the mold that produces penicillin.”

Academic tone and clarity
Some phrases are slightly informal or imprecise:
“wonder-drug” is vivid, but a bit journalistic. In a formal essay, you could say “a highly effective early antibiotic.”
“all kinds of antibiotics” is vague. Use a more precise phrase like “multiple classes of antibiotics.”

Suggested revision strategy
When revising, try this:
Write a stronger thesis with 2–3 clear claims.
Make each body paragraph support one claim.
Add at least one specific example in each body paragraph.
Correct scientific inaccuracies.
Reduce passive voice and revise repetitive sentence structures.

Overall
This essay has a solid foundation: the topic is clear, the historical development is understandable, and your scientific vocabulary is good. The next step is to make it more argumentative, more precise, and better supported with evidence. If you do that, your essay will become much stronger academically.

Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

Your essay has a clear topic and a strong overall structure. You identify one important 20th-century invention, explain its effects in several areas, and end with a clear conclusion. Your ideas are easy to follow, and you show good awareness that television has both positive and negative effects. This balance makes your argument more thoughtful.

Strengths

Clear focus  
   You stay on the topic of television throughout the essay.

Good organization  
   You discuss three main areas:
   - entertainment
   - communication
   - family life and values

   This gives your essay a logical structure.

Balanced argument  
   You do not only praise television; you also mention its harmful effects. This makes the essay more convincing.

Main Areas to Revise

Thesis and argument
Your thesis is interesting, but it could be more precise. Right now, the essay sometimes sounds descriptive rather than argumentative. Since this is an argumentative essay, make your position stronger.

For example, your introduction could make a clearer claim:
Current idea: television changed people’s lives.
Stronger argument: television is one of the most influential inventions of the 20th century because it transformed entertainment, improved access to information, and reshaped family relationships, despite some harmful effects.

This kind of thesis tells the reader exactly what you will argue.

Development of ideas
Some points need more explanation or examples.

For instance:
“television has become a major source for fast & accurate communicating”  
  This is a good point, but you should explain how. Do you mean live news, emergency broadcasts, or international events shown immediately?

“This creates problems with values which in turn, creates problems in society.”  
  This is a strong claim, so it needs support. Give an example or explain the connection more clearly.

Try to develop each body paragraph with:
a topic sentence
explanation
one or two specific examples
a concluding sentence

Grammar and word choice
There are many language errors, but most do not block understanding. Focus especially on these common problems:

a. Confusing words
their / there / they’re
  - “Of course their have been” → “Of course there have been”
  - “As television programing improves their is” → “there is”

b. Verb forms
“changed our way of live” → “changed our way of life”
“the become desensitized” → “they become desensitized”
“and and diminished” → remove repetition

c. Subject-verb agreement
“The types of programs is another issue” → “The types of programs are another issue”
“This creates problems with values which in turn, creates problems in society”  
  Better: “This creates problems with values, which in turn create problems in society.”

d. Word form and spelling
“20 century” → “20th century”
“come in to our homes” → “come into our homes”
“programing” → “programming”
“communicating” → better: “communication”
“news forms” → “new forms”
“yonkies” is not correct English. You could say:
  - “addicted to television”
  - “couch potatoes”
  - “people with their eyes glued to the screen”

Formal academic style
Your essay sometimes uses informal language or symbols that are not suitable for academic writing.

Avoid:
“&” → use and
“T.V.” → better to use television consistently
“it is anyone’s guess” → too informal for academic writing

Also, “fast & accurate communicating” sounds awkward. Better:
“a fast and accurate source of communication”
“a quick way of receiving information”

Sentence clarity and punctuation
Some sentences are too long or joined awkwardly. Use commas carefully, and consider splitting long sentences into two.

Example:
“Before people could only listen to the radio in their homes and they needed to go out to the theater in order to enjoy visual forms of entertainment.”
  
Better:
“Before television, people could only listen to the radio at home. They had to go to the theater to enjoy visual entertainment.”

This revision is clearer and more natural.

Suggested Revision Priorities

Correct grammar errors with there/their, verb forms, and plural agreement.
Make your thesis more argumentative.
Replace informal words and symbols with formal academic language.
Add more specific examples to support your claims.
Proofread for spelling and repeated words.

Final Comment

You have a solid foundation for a good essay. Your main ideas are relevant, and your organization is already strong. The biggest improvements now are in language accuracy, formal style, and stronger support for your claims. If you revise carefully, this essay can become much more persuasive and polished.

Feedback Report

You have a clear argument and an engaging, lively voice. Your essay has a strong central idea: labor-saving machines, especially washing machines, make life easier but may also contribute to a less active lifestyle. This is a good argumentative topic, and your use of rhetorical questions helps create interest. For revision, focus on making the argument more formal, more precise, and better organized.

Main strengths
Clear position: You argue that modern inventions are convenient but may reduce physical activity.
Interesting tone: The essay is vivid and easy to read.
Good use of examples: The washing machine is a concrete example that helps readers understand your point.

Main issues to revise

A. Make the thesis more direct
Your main claim appears only gradually. In an argumentative essay, it is better to state it clearly at the end of the introduction.

For example, you could revise the opening to something like:
> Although washing machines and other household appliances have greatly improved convenience, they have also encouraged a more sedentary lifestyle and increased dependence on technology.

This gives readers a clear roadmap.

B. Use a more formal academic style
Some expressions are too exaggerated, humorous, or informal for academic writing. For example:
“millions and millions”
“furiously running about in the streets”
“What ever would a college student do”
“&”
“....”

These create a dramatic style, but in academic argument they weaken credibility. Try to replace them with neutral, precise language.

Examples:
Informal: “millions and millions of lives were made simpler”
More academic: “the washing machine made household labor significantly easier for many families”

Also, avoid “man” to mean all humans. It sounds outdated. Use:
“humans”
“people”
“modern society”

C. Develop the argument with evidence
Right now, the essay mostly gives opinions and dramatic images. To be more convincing, explain why reduced household labor may affect health or behavior. You do not need research in every sentence, but you should give clearer reasoning.

For example, you could develop points like:
Washing machines reduce time and physical effort spent on chores.
Many modern appliances have similarly reduced daily movement.
As a result, people may need to exercise intentionally rather than naturally through daily labor.
However, convenience also has major social benefits, especially for workers, students, and caregivers.

This would make your argument more balanced and thoughtful.

D. Improve paragraph structure
Each paragraph should have one clear main idea.

Current paragraphs sometimes mix humor, exaggeration, and argument. A clearer structure could be:

Introduction: background + thesis  
Body paragraph 1: washing machines make life easier  
Body paragraph 2: dependence on appliances and loss of physical labor  
Body paragraph 3: counterargument—convenience is valuable, especially for busy people  
Conclusion: restate argument in a balanced way

E. Avoid overgeneralization
Some claims are too broad:
“most teenagers, students, & adults alike”
“Many housewives would insist”
“weight watchers ever an issue?”

Be careful with statements about “most people” or “today’s society” unless you can support them. Also, “housewives” is a narrow and somewhat outdated term; consider “many people responsible for household work.”

Language corrections
Here are some important sentence-level issues:

continously → continuously
necesity → necessity
with out → without
What ever → Whatever
handclothes washing → washing clothes by hand / hand-washing clothes
washing board → washboard
aid us in our modern process of rushing around → awkward; try help us manage busy modern lives
Avoid &; use and
Avoid repeated his/her; use them in modern academic English

Example revision:
> Give an average adult a washboard and soap and ask them to do a load of laundry, and their reaction may reveal how dependent many people have become on modern appliances.

Suggested revision of the conclusion
Your conclusion raises an interesting point, but it should sound more balanced and less sarcastic. For example:
> In conclusion, the washing machine is one of many inventions that have made daily life more convenient. However, this convenience has also reduced the physical effort once built into everyday tasks. Modern technology is not harmful in itself, but people should recognize that labor-saving devices may require us to seek physical activity in other ways.

Final advice
When you revise, try to:
state your thesis clearly
reduce exaggeration
use formal academic vocabulary
organize each paragraph around one main idea
support your ideas with explanation, not only opinion

You have a strong basic idea and a readable style. With a clearer structure and a more academic tone, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay is lively, personal, and easy to follow. You clearly take a strong position, and your examples make the topic memorable. The tone is engaging, and the discussion of smell, freshness, and travel shows that you understand how to support an argument with practical details. This is a good foundation for revision.

What you do well

Clear opinion
Your main claim is easy to understand: the zip-lock bag is an invention you cannot live without. This gives the essay a clear focus.

Strong use of examples
You do not only make general claims; you explain *how* zip-lock bags help in daily life. The sandwich-in-the-locker example is especially effective because it is concrete and amusing.

Good organization by function
Your body paragraphs are organized around different uses:
keeping food fresh
controlling odors
travel and other practical uses

This structure helps readers follow your reasoning.

Main areas to improve

Make the essay more formal
Some expressions sound too conversational for an academic argumentative essay. For example:
“It is funny that we are writing on this subject”
“I’m going to have to agree”
“What more could you ask for?”
“A third solution would be to bag everything!”

These are natural in speech, but in formal writing, try a more direct style. You can still keep your personal voice, but make it more polished.

Example revision of the introduction:
> Among the many useful modern inventions, the zip-lock bag is one of the most practical. It is indispensable because it preserves food, contains odors, and helps organize and protect items during travel.

This version gives your argument immediately and sounds more academic.

Strengthen your thesis
Your introduction currently begins with a personal story, but the main argument appears a little late. In an argumentative essay, your thesis should clearly state your position and main reasons.

A strong thesis might include your three main points:
preserves freshness
prevents odors
has many household and travel uses

Improve paragraph unity
Some paragraphs contain too many ideas at once. For example, the paragraph beginning “But, there are other uses…” includes ice, shampoo, Q-tips, pantyhose, earrings, shaving cream, and bathing suits. These examples are useful, but too many in one sentence make it hard to read.

You could split this into two paragraphs:
one on medical or household convenience
one on travel organization and protection

Watch grammar and word form
There are several small language errors. Here are some important ones:

“Anything can be put in zip-lock bag”  
  → “Anything can be put in a zip-lock bag”  
  You need an article: *a*.

“to bring to school to lunch”  
  → “to bring to school for lunch”

“foods to which they are near”  
  → “foods near them” or “foods that are nearby”  
  Your original sentence is grammatical, but unnatural.

“these baggies are even better”  
  “Baggies” is informal. Use “these bags” in academic writing.

“Gallon size zip-lock bags, solve this problem.”  
  → “Gallon-size zip-lock bags solve this problem.”  
  No comma is needed, and *gallon-size* should be hyphenated.

“the earing”  
  → “the earring”

“dry-clean only's”  
  → “dry-clean-only clothes” or “dry-clean-only garments”  
  Avoid unclear invented plural forms.

“the bathing suit that’s too wet to pack”  
  This is fine, but in a formal list, parallel structure matters.

Improve parallel structure
In the long list near the end, the grammar becomes uneven:
> “the Q-tips that you want to keep dry, the pantyhose you do not want to snag, the earing you do not want to lose one of…”

This can be revised for smoother parallelism:
> “Zip-lock bags can keep Q-tips dry, protect pantyhose from snagging, prevent the loss of earrings, and contain shaving cream or wet bathing suits during travel.”

Add a stronger conclusion
Your conclusion restates the main point, but it could be more persuasive by summarizing the main reasons more clearly.

Possible revision:
> In conclusion, the zip-lock bag is a simple but highly valuable invention. It keeps food fresh, prevents unwanted odors, and protects a wide range of items at home and while traveling. For these reasons, it is one of the most practical inventions in everyday life.

Revision priorities
Rewrite the introduction with a clear thesis.
Make the tone more formal.
Divide the long “other uses” paragraph.
Correct grammar, articles, spelling, and punctuation.
Revise the conclusion to summarize your argument more effectively.

Overall, this is an interesting and effective draft with vivid examples. With a more formal style and some sentence-level corrections, it can become a strong argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that the computer is the most influential invention of the 20th century. Logical organization

   - description of early computers and later development
   - explanation of social impact
   - prediction about the future  
   This progression is coherent.

Strong tone

productivity

These are good points, but each needs an example. You could mention:

schools using computers for research and learning
hospitals using computers to store medical records
the internet allowing fast global communication

Adding concrete examples will make your argument much stronger.

Improve paragraph development
Your third paragraph contains many ideas at once: business, information, productivity, the 21st century, personal computers, digital assistants, and the future. This makes the paragraph feel too long and unfocused.

Try dividing it into two paragraphs:

another paragraph about their future influence

This will improve clarity.

Some claims are too broad, for example:
“the productivity of people has increased ten-fold”
“every human being in the developed world will have their own PC”
“small digital assistants that will replace the printed page”

These statements sound interesting, but they need evidence or more careful wording. In academic writing, it is better to avoid absolute claims unless you can prove them.

“Computers have greatly increased productivity in many fields.”

“We are limited only by our imaginations” needs a period:
  - “We are limited only by our imaginations.”

Also, avoid unnecessary quotation marks:
“bright” indeed → just bright indeed

Strengthen academic style
Some expressions are vivid but slightly informal or dramatic for an academic essay:
“expensive monsters”
“20th century miracle”
“the day is just around the corner”

These phrases are not wrong, but they sound more conversational. For a stronger academic tone, consider:
“very large and expensive machines”
“an important technological development”
“it is likely that”

Introduce the 20th century and technological change

Body Paragraph 1

Body Paragraph 2
Show how computers changed work and business, with examples

Body Paragraph 3
Show how computers changed everyday life and access to information

Conclusion
Restate why computers were the most influential invention
Briefly mention their continuing importance

This is a promising essay with a clear central idea and good organization.
