Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: AIDS changed society’s attitudes, and education and acceptance are more important than fear and discrimination. This is a strong and meaningful argument. You also use concrete examples, such as Ryan White and Magic Johnson, which help make the essay more persuasive. Below are suggestions to help you revise.

Main argument and focus
Your central idea is good, but the essay’s thesis should be stated more clearly and precisely in the introduction.

Current opening:
> “One of the most important discoveries of the 20th Century would have to be AIDS.”

This sentence is problematic because AIDS was not a “discovery” in the usual sense; it is a disease that was identified or recognized. Also, your essay is not really about the medical discovery itself, but about social reactions to AIDS.

A clearer thesis could be:
“The emergence of AIDS in the late twentieth century changed public attitudes toward sexuality, health, and mortality, while also exposing prejudice and the need for better education and compassion.”

This would better match the content of your essay.

Organization
Your essay generally follows a logical order:
Early reactions to AIDS  
Increased public concern when children were affected  
Public education and Ryan White  
Need for acceptance and education

This structure works, but the paragraphs could be connected more smoothly. Try to make each paragraph focus on one main point.

For example:
Paragraph 1: Early prejudice and indifference
Paragraph 2: Public fear and education after Ryan White
Paragraph 3: Why education and acceptance are still necessary

Adding topic sentences will improve clarity.

Use of evidence and tone
You make strong claims, but some are too general or too emotional. In academic writing, it is better to avoid overgeneralization.

Examples:
“no one really cared that gays would die”
“no one really cared”
“Children are becoming sexually active at the age of eleven”

These statements are very broad and may not be accurate for all people. Instead, qualify them:
“Many people showed indifference toward those affected, especially gay men and intravenous drug users.”
“Public concern increased significantly when children became infected.”
“Some children become sexually active at a very young age.”

This makes your argument sound more balanced and credible.

Grammar and word choice
There are several language issues to revise.

A. Word choice
“effect them” → “affect them”  
  “Affect” is usually the verb; “effect” is usually a noun.

“discovery of AIDS” → “emergence of AIDS” or “identification of AIDS”

“contract AIDS” is acceptable, though today people often say “be infected with HIV” or “develop AIDS”, depending on meaning.

B. Articles and hyphenation
“a twelve year old hemophiliac” → “a twelve-year-old hemophiliac”
“20th Century” → “20th century” (usually lowercase in running text)

C. Sentence problems
This is a sentence fragment:
> “Even if a cure is never found.”

It should be connected to the next sentence:
“Even if a cure is never found, AIDS victims should be accepted and given the respect they deserve.”

D. Informal abbreviations
In academic writing, avoid:
“t.v.” → “television”
“&” → “and”
“80's” → “1980s”

Style and academic register
Your writing is passionate, which is good, but some expressions are too informal for an academic essay.

Examples:
“How wrong they were.”
“It’s too bad that...”
“grab the attention of the public”

Possible revisions:
“This assumption proved to be dangerously false.”
“It is unfortunate that...”
“attract public attention”

Try to use a more formal tone throughout.

Historical and conceptual accuracy
Be careful with terminology. AIDS and HIV are related but not identical. If possible, use the terms accurately:
HIV = the virus
AIDS = the advanced condition caused by HIV

Also, saying “children became infected with AIDS” is medically imprecise. Better:
“children became infected with HIV” or “children developed AIDS.”

Suggested revision strategy
When you rewrite, try this:
Write a more precise thesis.
Replace broad claims with qualified statements.
Use formal academic language.
Correct grammar and punctuation.
Add smoother transitions between paragraphs.

Final comment
This essay shows strong engagement with an important topic and a clear moral purpose. Your main strengths are your clear viewpoint, relevant examples, and concern for social justice. The next step is to make your argument more academic by improving precision, tone, and sentence control. With revision, this can become a much stronger argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear personal voice and an original position: you argue that medical inventions are the only twentieth-century inventions that have truly improved human life, while many other inventions harm nature and enable domination. This is an interesting and ambitious argument. Your writing also shows strong engagement with the topic, which is a good foundation for revision.

Strengths

Clear viewpoint  
   Your main claim is easy to identify. The reader understands that you value medical inventions and are skeptical of other kinds of technological progress.

Thoughtful, philosophical tone  
   You go beyond simple examples and try to explain *why* humans invent things. This gives your essay depth.

Logical contrast  
   The contrast between harmful inventions and helpful medical inventions helps organize your ideas.

Main areas to improve

Make the thesis more precise and arguable
Your thesis is interesting, but it is currently too absolute:

> “the only inventions of the twentieth century that have significantly changed people's lives for the better are those that have occured in the medical field”

This is a very strong claim, so the reader expects strong evidence. It may be difficult to defend because many non-medical inventions also improved life, such as sanitation systems, household electricity, or communication technology.

Suggestion: make your argument more precise, for example:
“Among twentieth-century inventions, medical advances have had the most clearly positive effect on human life.”
“While many inventions brought mixed consequences, medical inventions improved people’s lives most directly.”

This keeps your opinion strong but easier to support.

Use more concrete evidence
Most of the essay is abstract. You discuss “nature,” “fear of death,” and “domination,” but you do not give enough examples. Argumentative essays become stronger when claims are supported by specific evidence.

Add examples such as:
antibiotics
vaccines
MRI or X-ray technology
insulin
surgical techniques

Also, if you say other inventions are harmful, give examples:
nuclear weapons
industrial pollution
technologies that increase environmental destruction

Without examples, the essay sounds more like a reflection than a convincing argument.

Improve paragraph development
Each body paragraph should have:
a topic sentence
an explanation
one or two examples
a link back to the thesis

Right now, some paragraphs are very short, especially the third and fourth paragraphs. For instance:

> “In addition, our inventions are used to dominate one another.”

This idea needs development. Explain *how*, give examples, and connect it to your main claim.

Consider counterarguments
A strong argumentative essay often recognizes the other side. For example, someone might argue that transportation, computers, or communication technologies have also improved human life. You do not need to agree, but briefly mentioning and responding to this point would make your essay more persuasive.

For example:
“Although inventions such as computers and automobiles have made life more convenient, they have also contributed to pollution, isolation, or inequality.”

Language and style corrections

Here are some important corrections:

occured → occurred
affect (noun) → effect  
  In your sentence, you need: “a negative effect on our lives”
truely → truly
0the century → probably 20th century or twentieth century
Remove extra spaces before punctuation:
  - “For these reasons ,” → “For these reasons,”

Sentence-level suggestions

Original:
> “Interestingly enough, this topic is one that is very important to me.”

This opening is personal, but a stronger academic opening would directly introduce the argument.

Possible revision:
> “Twentieth-century inventions have transformed human life, but not all of these changes have been beneficial.”

Original:
> “We continue to destroy the plants, animals, and the earth itself and our new inventions help us to do it in more efficient ways.”

This is a strong idea, but you could make it more formal:
> “Modern inventions often make environmental destruction faster and more efficient.”

Original:
> “They are one of the only truely ‘kind’ expressions of mankind’s creativity.”

Because they refers to plural inventions, this should be:
> “They are among the only truly ‘kind’ expressions of human creativity.”

Organization suggestion

   - Give examples

Body Paragraph 3

   - Give specific examples of benefits

Conclusion

   - End with a broader reflection

Final advice

adding specific examples
developing each paragraph more fully
correcting spelling and word choice errors

You have a thoughtful argument; now focus on supporting it with evidence and clearer structure.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a strong sense that the satellite dish changed modern life in politics, business, and entertainment. You also try to discuss both positive and negative effects, which is good for an argumentative essay. To revise effectively, focus on (1) thesis and argument, (2) organization, (3) evidence and logic, and (4) language accuracy.

Thesis and argument
Right now, your main claim is not precise enough. The essay seems to argue that the satellite dish has had a major global impact, but your final position is unclear because you end by saying readers must decide for themselves.

In an argumentative essay, you should present a clear, debatable thesis. For example:

Stronger thesis: “Although satellite dishes have contributed to excessive entertainment consumption and military spending, they have overall benefited society by improving global communication, news access, and international business.”

This gives your essay a direction and lets each paragraph support that claim.

Organization
Your essay has an introduction, body paragraphs, and a conclusion, but the body needs clearer structure. Some paragraphs are too short, and some ideas are not fully explained.

A possible structure:

Introduction
   - Introduce the satellite dish
   - Explain its importance
   - State your thesis clearly

Body Paragraph 1: Politics and news
   - Explain how satellite technology allows fast international news transmission
   - Use CNN as an example
   - Explain why this matters for governments and citizens

Body Paragraph 2: Business and the economy
   - Show how global communication supports trade, finance, and company decisions
   - Explain the connection more clearly

Body Paragraph 3: Entertainment
   - Discuss MTV, cable TV, and global media culture
   - Explain both economic benefits and possible negative social effects

Body Paragraph 4: Negative effects
   - Military spending, overconsumption of television, or misinformation
   - Be specific and support claims

Conclusion
   - Restate your argument
   - Summarize main reasons
   - End with a strong final judgment

Evidence and logic
This is the area that most needs revision. Some claims are interesting, but they need support or clearer explanation. For example:

“40% of U.S. millionares are entertainers.”
“The intelligence of an average teenager is lowering itself due to cable viewing.”
“Naval programs have wasted exceeding amounts of money…”

These statements sound too broad and are not supported by evidence. In academic writing, avoid unsupported statistics or sweeping generalizations. Either provide reliable evidence or make the claim more cautious.

For example:

Instead of “The intelligence of an average teenager is lowering itself,” write:
  - “Some critics argue that excessive television viewing may reduce time spent on reading or study.”

Also, some cause-and-effect links are too direct. For example, CNN was not simply “created by satellite dish”; rather, satellite technology helped make global broadcasting possible. Try to show more careful reasoning.

Language accuracy and style
You have many ideas, but grammar and word choice problems sometimes make the meaning unclear. Below are common issues to revise:

A. Capitalization
Use capitals only for proper nouns:
Panasonic, not “panasonic”
CNN, MTV
satellite dish, not “Satellite dish” in the middle of a sentence
information age, usually not capitalized unless part of a title

B. Word choice and spelling
Please correct:
“it’s center” → its center
“recieve” → receive
“bases” → basis
“affect” → effect (in “have had a strong effect”)
“millionares” → millionaires
“people lives” → people’s lives
“pro’s and con’s” → pros and cons

C. Grammar
Examples:
“created in the last 0 years” → probably created in the last 20 years
“that nights news” → that night’s news
“such a STARWARS” → such as Star Wars
“The only observation which can be made, is that:” → remove unnecessary comma and colon

D. Sentence clarity
Some sentences are too long or awkward. For example:

Original:
> “A large, 1/2 egg, sometimes as large as a small FIAT, with a metal arrow in it's center has changed the size of the world...”

This opening is vivid, but confusing. Consider a simpler academic style:
> “The satellite dish, though physically small, has transformed global communication.”

Final advice
When revising, focus first on content and structure, then on grammar and proofreading. Your essay has a promising central topic, but it will become much stronger if you:
state a clear position,
organize paragraphs by main idea,
support claims with evidence or cautious reasoning,
edit for grammar and academic style.

You have a good foundation; now work on making your argument clearer, more formal, and better supported.

Strong historical perspective

Engaging voice  
   Phrases such as “we are open to manipulation as well as stimulation” are memorable and effective.

Main areas to improve

Strengthen the thesis and topic sentences
Your thesis is interesting, but some wording is unclear or too broad. For example:

“mass culturization of various grupes”
“the rapidity and breath of the information”

These phrases should be made more precise. For example:

“the creation of a more uniform mass culture”

This makes your argument more credible and balanced.

Use a more formal academic style

“&”
“w/”
“N.Y.”

“and”
“with”
“New York”

Work on grammar, spelling, and punctuation
There are many language errors that interfere with clarity. Common issues include:

Spelling: “grupes” → “groups”; “breath” → “breadth”; “lead” → “led”; “livingrooms” → “living rooms”; “inhance” → “enhance”
Capitalization: “united States” → “United States”; “vietnam war” → “Vietnam War”
Sentence structure: “The television a powerful and dangerous tool.” → “Television is a powerful and dangerous tool.”
Possessives/plurals: “peoples ability” → “people’s ability”
Articles: “the television” is sometimes unnatural. Usually just say “television” when speaking generally.

Be especially careful with comma splices and run-on sentences. Example:

“Our view of the world is different, it's closer, it's in our livingrooms.”
  
Better:
“Our view of the world is different: it feels closer because it is brought into our living rooms.”

Develop paragraph coherence
Some ideas within paragraphs jump too quickly. For example, in the first body paragraph, you move from 1950s family sitcoms to race and “ghetto life” without enough transition. Try this pattern in each body paragraph:

topic sentence
explanation
example
analysis of the example
link back to thesis

This will make your reasoning easier to follow.

Suggested revision priorities

Revise the introduction so the thesis is clearer and more formal.
Replace informal abbreviations and symbols with standard academic English.
Edit for grammar and spelling carefully.
Qualify broad claims with words like “many,” “often,” “in some cases,” or “to some extent.”
Expand analysis after examples so you explain not only what television showed, but why that mattered.

Example of a stronger sentence
Original:
“This abundance of information has lead to a drastic alteration in peoples ability to react to major events.”

Revised:
“This abundance of information has led to a major change in people’s ability to respond to national and global events.”

Final comment
This essay has a strong central idea and good supporting examples. Your main challenge is not content but academic expression: clearer wording, more precise claims, and more careful grammar. If you revise sentence by sentence for formality and correctness, your argument will become much more powerful.

   Restate your argument and explain why the event is still remembered.

Your essay is persuasive in tone, but it needs more concrete historical support. At present, some claims are too general, such as “the people had suffered under a communist regime” or “communism as we once knew it no longer exists.”

reforms in Eastern Europe,
protests in Leipzig,
Hungary opening its border with Austria,
the Velvet Revolution in Czechoslovakia,
the collapse of Romanian communism.

These details will make your argument stronger and more academic.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that television was the most important twentieth-century invention because it changed how people receive news, entertainment, and global information.    - This gives the essay a strong central idea.

Good comparison strategy

   - This helps readers understand *why* television was different and more influential.

Balanced argument
   - You do not only praise television; you also mention its power to persuade and the need to control it.
   - This makes the essay more thoughtful and mature.

Main areas to improve

For example:

This version gives the reader a clear roadmap for the body paragraphs.

Strengthen paragraph unity

topic sentence
explanation
example
link back to thesis

For instance, your paragraph about radio is descriptive, but it should connect more directly to your argument about why television was superior.

Develop examples more specifically
You often make broad claims, but the essay would be stronger with concrete examples. For example:
television coverage of wars
televised political speeches or debates
educational programs
live sports broadcasts

Instead of saying “The examples could be endless,” choose one or two detailed examples. Specific evidence makes arguments more persuasive.

Improve word choice and formal style
There are several informal or inaccurate word choices. Examples:
“literal explosion” → too exaggerated; try “rapid growth”
“peak” → should be “peek”, but in formal writing better: “glimpse”
“the worlds together” → better: “bring the world closer together”
“spening” → “spending”

There are many sentence-level problems, especially with:
singular/plural nouns
possessives
articles
prepositions
spelling

“absorbtion” → “absorption”
“olympic” → “Olympic”

Shorter sentences will make your meaning clearer.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a strong overall position: the computer is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed everyday life and business. The writing shows good awareness of historical development and includes useful examples such as writing papers, doing taxes, and accessing information. To revise effectively, focus on making your argument more precise, improving organization, and correcting grammar/style issues.

Main strengths

Clear central idea  
   Your essay consistently argues that computers have had a major impact on society.

Logical general structure  
   You move from the past (early computers), to the personal computer revolution, to its effects on society and the future.

Relevant examples  
   Examples such as databases, taxes, portable computers, and business use help show practical impact.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more specific
Your introduction gives the topic, but the thesis is a little long and unclear.

Current idea:
> “The computer… has had a great impact on how society and especially business gathers, creates, responds to and, understands everyday information.”

This can be clearer:
> Among 20th-century inventions, the personal computer has had the greatest impact because it changed how people work, organize information, and communicate.

This version is more direct and easier to support in the body paragraphs.

B. Improve paragraph focus
Some body paragraphs are too long and include many ideas at once. Try to give one main idea per paragraph.

A possible organization:
Introduction: importance of computers + thesis
Paragraph 1: early computers and why society needed them
Paragraph 2: the personal computer made computing affordable and available at home
Paragraph 3: computers improved communication and access to information
Conclusion: computers transformed life and will remain important

This will make your argument easier to follow.

C. Use a more formal academic style
Some phrases are too conversational or repetitive, for example:
“just about anything”
“a very powerful tool indeed”
“full force to dominate our everyday lives”

Try using more formal language:
“a wide range of tasks”
“an extremely powerful tool”
“began a technological revolution”

Also, the essay repeats “information” and “everyday” many times. Repetition makes the writing less effective. Use synonyms where possible:
information → data, knowledge, materials, records
everyday → daily, ordinary, routine

D. Develop the argument, not only description
Much of the essay describes what computers do, but an argumentative essay should also explain why this proves your claim. For example, after describing home computers, add a sentence like:
> “This accessibility was revolutionary because it transferred computing power from governments and large companies to ordinary individuals.”

That kind of explanation strengthens your argument.

Grammar and language issues

A. Subject-verb agreement
“There has been many inventions” → “There have been many inventions”

B. Article use
As a Chinese speaker, article use (“a,” “an,” “the”) may be especially challenging. Check these examples:
“with the use of Modem” → “with the use of a modem”
“through the phone on the other computers” → “through telephone lines to other computers”

C. Word form and spelling
“recieve” → “receive”
“Modem” should not be capitalized unless at the start of a sentence.

D. Sentence clarity
Some sentences are too long and awkward. For example:
> “What the PC (personal computer) meant for society was an affordable tool that would help one sort out, save, delete or add and receive information in one's own home.”

A clearer version:
> “The personal computer gave people an affordable way to store, organize, edit, and receive information at home.”

E. Pronoun consistency
You shift between “one,” “you,” “we,” and “society.” In formal essays, try to stay consistent. For example, use “people” or “users” instead of changing pronouns.

Sample revision of the introduction

Original:
> There has been many inventions in the 20th century that have changed our everyday lives.

Possible revision:
> The 20th century produced many inventions that transformed daily life. Among them, the personal computer has had the greatest impact because it revolutionized the way people work, manage information, and communicate.

Final advice for revision

When you revise, focus on these priorities:

Write a clear, direct thesis
Divide long paragraphs into focused body paragraphs
Use formal academic vocabulary
Explain why your examples support your argument
Edit carefully for grammar, articles, spelling, and sentence clarity

You have a strong foundation and a meaningful topic. With clearer structure and more precise language, this essay can become much more persuasive and polished.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear central idea: the 20th century can be called “The Century of Democracy.” This is a strong and interesting argument, and your voice is confident and engaged. The personal tone (“I feel lucky,” “I’m feeling very patriotic”) makes the essay lively and sincere. You also use historical examples, which is good for argumentative writing. To revise, focus mainly on thesis clarity, organization, evidence, and language accuracy.

Strengths
Clear topic and strong opinion: You consistently argue that democracy is the most important development of the 20th century.
Engaging introduction: The opening creates historical atmosphere and catches the reader’s attention.
Personal voice: Your reflections make the essay feel authentic.
Use of examples: Germany, the Soviet Union, and the end of the Cold War are relevant examples.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more direct
Your thesis appears at the end of the first paragraph, but the introduction is quite long and general. In an argumentative essay, it is better to present the main claim more directly.

For example:
> As the year 2000 approaches, I believe the 20th century should be remembered as “the Century of Democracy” because democratic movements reshaped nations, ended political divisions, and created hope for more peaceful international relations.

This version tells the reader exactly what your argument will be.

B. Improve organization
Right now, some paragraphs feel like personal reflections rather than clear argument paragraphs. Try this structure:

Introduction + thesis
Paragraph 1: Democracy in Germany
Paragraph 2: Democracy in the former Soviet Union
Paragraph 3: Democracy and world peace / end of Cold War
Conclusion

Each body paragraph should begin with a topic sentence that clearly supports the thesis.

Example:
> One reason the 20th century deserves to be called the “Century of Democracy” is that democratic movements helped reunify Germany.

C. Use more precise and accurate evidence
Some claims are too broad or historically inaccurate.

Examples:
“East and West Germany… unite as one democratic superpower”  
  Germany reunited, but it is not usually called a “superpower.” Better:  
  > East and West Germany were reunified, symbolizing the decline of authoritarian division in Europe.
  
“The Soviet Union… rid their nation of its communist government”  
  This is too simplified. The process was complex and involved political reforms, economic crisis, and the collapse of the Soviet state. Try to describe it more carefully.

“The idea of a nuclear war is practically non-existent today”  
  This is too absolute. A better version:  
  > The end of the Cold War reduced the immediate fear of nuclear conflict.

In argumentative writing, avoid exaggeration and make your statements more balanced.

D. Keep the tone academic
Your essay is expressive, but some phrases are too informal or emotional for academic argument.

Examples:
“How could we ever forget…” → too conversational
“fabulous fifties” → informal
“I’m feeling very patriotic these days” → personal, but not very analytical

You can still include personal perspective, but balance it with formal analysis.

Language corrections

Here are some important sentence-level issues:

“The ‘Roaring 20’s’ for example was marked…”  
  → *The “Roaring Twenties,” for example, was marked…*  
  (“for example” needs commas; “20’s” should be “Twenties” or “1920s”)

“A very capricious decade, it can be contrasted…”  
  This is awkward because “it” is unclear.  
  → *This lively decade can be contrasted with the years of hunger and unemployment during the Great Depression of the 1930s.*

“The Soviet Union, as well is going through…”  
  → *The Soviet Union, as well, is going through…*

“marxism”  
  → *Marxism* (capitalize)

“its communist government”  
  This is grammatically fine, but be careful with political wording and historical precision.

“follow its happening”  
  → *witness its fall* or *see it happen*

“one bond- democracy-”  
  → *one bond—democracy—*  
  Use dashes correctly, or simply use commas.

Also watch:
article use (*the 20th century*, *a democracy*)
capitalization (*20th century* unless in a title)
comma spacing and punctuation

Revision advice
When revising, ask yourself:
What exactly am I trying to prove?
Does each paragraph give one clear reason?
Are my examples accurate and specific?
Is my tone more academic than conversational?

Final comment
This essay has a strong idea and genuine enthusiasm. Your biggest task is to make the argument more focused and formal. If you shorten the introduction, state your thesis more clearly, and develop each example in a more precise way, your essay will become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: nuclear energy is dangerous and people should take action against it. The writing also has a strong emotional tone, which helps show your concern. You use persuasive language effectively in places, especially in the conclusion. To improve the essay, focus on accuracy, logic, organization, and language control.

Main strengths
Clear argument: You consistently argue that nuclear energy is dangerous.
Strong voice: Phrases like “It starts with you” create a persuasive effect.
Good sense of urgency: The essay shows why the issue matters now.

Biggest content problem: confusing ideas
The essay mixes nuclear weapons and nuclear energy as if they are the same thing. This weakens your argument.

For example:
Paragraphs 1–2 discuss the atomic bomb.
Later paragraphs discuss nuclear power plants and environmental pollution.

These are related, but they are not the same issue. A bomb is a weapon; nuclear power is an energy source. If you want to argue against nuclear energy, you need to explain clearly:
what nuclear energy is,
how it is used,
what risks it creates,
and why those risks outweigh the benefits.

Also, some claims are factually weak or inaccurate, such as:
“blowing holes in our sky”
linking nuclear energy directly to “holes in our ozone layer”

The ozone hole is not mainly caused by nuclear energy. If your evidence is inaccurate, readers may doubt your whole argument.

Organization and development
Your essay has a beginning, middle, and end, but the ideas need a more logical structure.

A better structure might be:

Introduction
   - Introduce nuclear energy as a controversial invention.
   - State your thesis clearly.

Body paragraph 1: dangers
   - Explain risks such as accidents, radioactive waste, or weaponization.

Body paragraph 2: counterargument
   - Acknowledge why countries still use nuclear energy (high output, low carbon emissions).
   - Then explain why you still oppose it.

Body paragraph 3: solutions
   - Suggest safer alternatives such as solar, wind, and hydroelectric power.

Conclusion
   - Restate your argument and call for action.

Right now, several paragraphs repeat the same general idea (“nuclear energy is dangerous”) without adding enough new support.

Need for evidence
Your essay is very opinion-based. In academic argumentative writing, you need specific evidence:
examples of nuclear disasters (Chernobyl, Fukushima)
statistics about radioactive waste
comparisons with renewable energy sources
expert opinion or scientific findings

Without evidence, the essay sounds passionate but not fully convincing.

Language and grammar
There are several sentence-level issues. Below are some important examples:

a. Word choice
“The discovery of the Atomic energy” → “The discovery of atomic energy”
  - No need for “the,” and “atomic” should not be capitalized.
“awesome” may sound informal or ambiguous. Consider “powerful”, “significant”, or “formidable.”
“is routed in” → “is rooted in”
“augments” sounds unnatural here. Better: “increases” or “grows.”
“other various factors” is redundant. Use “various factors” or “other factors.”
“irreversable” → “irreversible”

b. Grammar
“the the most” → delete one “the”
“For this reason, It is considered…” → “it” should not be capitalized in the middle of a sentence.
“many other power source alternatives” → better: “many alternative energy sources”
“what is to become of our children.” → this should be a question: “What is to become of our children?”

c. Sentence style
Some sentences are dramatic but too broad or unclear. For example:
“rendering our seas useless”
This is too vague. Try to be more precise.

Tone and academic style
Your essay is persuasive, but at times it sounds more like a speech than an academic essay. For university writing, try to use a slightly more formal tone and avoid exaggeration unless you can support it with evidence.

For example:
“The lack of sight from our leaders is almost appalling.”
Better:
“The lack of foresight among world leaders is alarming.”

Possible thesis revision
Your current thesis is not fully clear. You could write something like:

“Although nuclear energy can provide large amounts of electricity, its risks—such as accidents, radioactive waste, and links to nuclear weapons—make it a dangerous choice that should be replaced by safer renewable energy sources.”

This thesis is more specific and easier to support.

Final advice
For revision, focus on these priorities:
Separate nuclear weapons from nuclear energy clearly.
Use accurate facts and examples.
Create a clearer paragraph structure.
Add evidence, not only opinion.
Edit grammar, word choice, and punctuation carefully.

You have a strong sense of purpose and a clear desire to persuade the reader. With more precise language and better-supported reasoning, this essay can become much more effective.

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that television is one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because it changed how people receive information and understand the world. Strengths

Relevant examples: The Vietnam War and Somalia are useful examples of television’s social impact.

Main areas to improve

A. Make the thesis more precise

For example, your thesis could be clearer like this:

This version tells the reader exactly what your main supporting points will be.

B. Improve paragraph unity and organization
Each body paragraph should have one clear main idea.

Paragraph 2 discusses war, global events, and comparison with radio. These ideas are related, but the paragraph would be stronger if the topic sentence clearly introduced the idea of television making distant events visible and emotionally powerful.
Paragraph 3 includes both the idea that television “shrinks the planet” and the negative effects of television. This makes the paragraph less focused. Consider separating the benefits and drawbacks into different paragraphs.

A possible structure:

Television influences public opinion and social action
Drawbacks of television
Conclusion

C. Support your points more fully
Some claims are interesting but not fully explained. For example:

“This was one of the factors that led to much of the protesting.”  
  → Explain how televised war images changed public opinion.

  → Give a clearer example of how repeated violent images may reduce emotional response.

Try to follow this pattern: point → example → explanation.

Language and grammar issues

A. Verb tense
You switch between present and past tense in ways that sound awkward.

“television had changed people’s lives”  
  Better: “television has changed people’s lives”
“The Vietnam War was the first time…” is correct because it refers to a past event.

B. Sentence structure
Some sentences are too long or awkwardly connected.

Original:
> There are, however, several downsides to the television, and, like all inventions can be used in a useless way.

Better:
> However, television also has several drawbacks, and like all inventions, it can be used in unproductive ways.

C. Word choice and natural expression
Some phrases sound unnatural in English.

“the television” → usually just “television” or “TV”
“make smaller the dimensions of the planet” → “made the world seem smaller”
“unknown and unheard of areas” → “unknown or remote places”
“used in a useless way” → “used in unproductive or harmful ways”

D. Capitalization
“vietnam war” should be “Vietnam War”

Sentence-level corrections

Here are a few suggested revisions:

Original: “This simple fact has changed in many ways how we live…”  
  Better: “This ability has changed the way we live in many ways…”

Original: “We are able also to see for ourselves what is happening around the globe…”  
  Better: “We are also able to see for ourselves what is happening around the globe…”

Original: “Gone are the days of unknown and unheard of areas.”  
  Better: “Television has reduced the sense that distant parts of the world are unknown to us.”

Final advice for revision

When you revise, focus on these priorities:

Write a sharper thesis
Give each paragraph one main idea
Add explanation after each example
Correct awkward phrases and grammar
Separate positive effects from negative effects more clearly

Overall, this is a promising essay with meaningful ideas. Your main argument is strong, but the essay will become much more convincing if you improve the organization and make your language more precise and natural.

Improve paragraph organization

For example:

Paragraph 1: Airplanes made long-distance travel faster and safer.

Use more formal academic style

Examples:
“opened doors to traveling” → *made travel more accessible*
“a completely impossible concept” → *something humans could not achieve on their own*

“The extend to which” → “The extent to which”
“long distance traveling” → “long-distance travel”

Although people can now communicate digitally, air travel remains essential to modern life.*

Suggested revision of one sentence
Original:

Improved:
*Before the invention of the airplane, long-distance travel was slow, difficult, and often dangerous.*

Final advice
For revision, focus on:
Writing a more specific thesis
Organizing each paragraph around one main point
Correcting grammar and word choice

You have a solid foundation and a meaningful topic. With clearer organization and more precise language, this essay can become much stronger.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0033.1.txt =====
Feedback Report on Your Argumentative Essay

You have chosen an important topic and your main idea is clear: education is essential in preventing AIDS, especially when medicine is not enough. This is a strong argument. Your essay also shows concern for public health and tries to persuade the reader, which is good for argumentative writing.

What you do well
Clear topic: The essay focuses on AIDS prevention.
Strong position: You argue that knowledge and public education can help prevent the disease.
Relevant examples: You mention schools, hospitals, drug use, and risky sexual behavior, which support your main point.

Main areas to improve

Create a clearer thesis
Your introduction gives background, but your main argument should be stated more directly. Right now, the essay suggests that education matters, but the thesis is not fully clear.

Possible revised thesis:
> Although modern medicine has made great progress, AIDS cannot be controlled by treatment alone; therefore, public education is one of the most effective ways to prevent its spread.

This sentence clearly tells the reader your position and the essay’s focus.

Improve organization
Your ideas are relevant, but the essay sometimes jumps from one point to another. Try this structure:

Introduction: background on AIDS + thesis
Body paragraph 1: why medicine alone is not enough
Body paragraph 2: how hospitals and schools can educate the public
Body paragraph 3: examples of risky behaviors and why awareness matters
Conclusion: restate thesis and emphasize importance of education

Each paragraph should begin with a topic sentence that connects to your thesis.

Use a more formal academic style
Avoid informal abbreviations and symbols such as:
& → use and
w/ → use with
contractions like it’s → use it is

Also, some phrases sound too conversational or emotional, such as “selling their lives short.” In academic writing, use more precise language.

Be careful with accuracy and tone
Some statements may sound unfair, outdated, or medically inaccurate. For example:
“victims of AIDS” is less appropriate than people living with AIDS or people with HIV/AIDS
“homosexuals are not the only possible carriers” may sound stigmatizing. It is better to focus on modes of transmission rather than groups of people.
Mentioning dentists who forget gloves is not a strong example unless you provide evidence.

Try to avoid language that blames certain groups. A stronger academic approach is to discuss behaviors and public health practices.

Strengthen grammar and sentence structure
There are several grammar problems that affect clarity. Here are some examples:

“the population has needed to seek”  
  Better: “people have needed to seek”
“Many schools have begun inviting victims of AIDS”  
  Better: “Many schools have begun inviting people living with AIDS”
“people who interact w/ many unknown sexual partners”  
  Better: “people who have multiple sexual partners”
“they interact sexual w/ complete strangers”  
  Better: “they have sexual contact with complete strangers”
“The AIDS viruses was discovered”  
  Better: “The AIDS virus was discovered” or “AIDS was identified”
“changed many peoples lives for the worst”  
  Better: “changed many people’s lives for the worse”

Use punctuation more carefully
You often use semicolons incorrectly. A semicolon should connect two closely related complete sentences. In many places, a comma or period would be better.

For example:
> “Due to the ineffectiveness of medicines; the population has needed…”

Better:
> “Due to the ineffectiveness of medicines, people have needed…”

Develop your evidence
Your argument would be much stronger if you explained how education helps. For example, education can teach:
how HIV is transmitted
how it is not transmitted
the importance of safe sex
the dangers of needle sharing
how reducing stigma encourages testing and treatment

Adding these points will make your essay more persuasive.

Example of a stronger revised sentence
Original:
> “If AIDS can not be prevented by medication, it is possible that it can be prevented by knowledge.”

Revised:
> “If medicine alone cannot stop the spread of AIDS, then public education may be one of the most effective tools for prevention.”

Final advice
When revising, focus on:
writing a clear thesis
organizing each paragraph around one main point
using formal academic language
correcting grammar and punctuation
avoiding stigmatizing or inaccurate statements
adding clearer explanations and evidence

You already have a meaningful central idea. With better structure and more careful language, this essay can become much stronger and more convincing.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear personal voice and a strong opinion. It is lively, easy to follow, and your examples make the topic feel real. The reader can understand why contact lenses matter to you. This is a good strength in argumentative writing: you do not only state your opinion, but also explain it with personal experience.

What you do well

Clear position
   You clearly argue that contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century.

Good use of personal examples
   Your examples about basketball, swimming, and waking up in the morning help the reader understand the practical benefits of contact lenses.

Engaging tone
   The essay sounds natural and energetic. Questions like “How would I be able to see while swimming...?” help involve the reader.

Main areas to improve

Make the essay more argumentative
Right now, the essay is mostly personal reflection. That is effective, but an argumentative essay should also explain why this invention is important for society in general, not only for you.

For example, you could add points such as:
contact lenses improve quality of life for millions of people
they help people work, study, and play sports more easily
they offer an alternative for people who cannot comfortably wear glasses
they show how medical technology can improve daily life

This will make your argument broader and stronger.

Use a more formal academic style
Some expressions are too informal for an academic essay. For example:
“I’d be lost without”
“a pain to clean”
“I can’t even begin to explain”
“who am I to say!”
“glasses are a thing of the past!”

These phrases sound conversational. In academic writing, use a more neutral tone.

Examples:
“I would find daily life much more difficult without them.”
“Hard lenses were often uncomfortable and difficult to maintain.”
“Disposable lenses represented a major improvement in convenience.”
“Although colored lenses may seem unnecessary to some people, they remain popular among consumers.”

Improve organization
Your essay has an introduction, body, and conclusion, but the structure can be clearer.

A possible structure:
Introduction: introduce the topic and thesis
Body paragraph 1: limits of glasses
Body paragraph 2: advantages of soft lenses
Body paragraph 3: advantages of disposable lenses
Body paragraph 4: broader social importance
Conclusion: restate your argument

Also, your paragraph about colored lenses feels disconnected from the main argument. Either explain how it supports your point, or remove it.

Correct grammar and word choice
There are several language issues that need revision.

Subject-verb agreement
“a style called ‘hard lenses’ were the only kind made”  
  → “a style called ‘hard lenses’ was the only kind made”

Word form/spelling
“lense craze” → “lens craze”
“contact lense case” → “contact lens case”

Prepositions and phrasing
“my only form of seeing better”  
  → “my only way of seeing better” or “my only form of vision correction”
“caught rapidly”  
  → “spread rapidly” or “became popular rapidly”

Tense consistency
You shift between present and past in some places. Try to keep time relationships clear.

Avoid symbols such as “&”
In formal writing, write “and” instead of “&”. Also, be careful with punctuation spacing and repeated exclamation marks. These make the essay look less polished.

For example:
“uncomfortable & a pain to clean & insert”  
  → “uncomfortable and difficult to clean and insert”

Strengthen the thesis
Your first sentence is interesting, but your thesis can be more precise. For example:

Possible revised thesis:
“Contact lenses are one of the most important inventions of the 20th century because they have improved comfort, mobility, and independence for people with poor vision.”

This gives the reader a clear map of your main points.

Example of a more formal revision of one sentence
Original:
“Once ‘soft lenses’ were introduced to the public, the ‘lense craze’ caught rapidly & hasn’t died down, yet.”

Revised:
“Once soft lenses were introduced, their popularity spread rapidly and has remained strong ever since.”

Final advice
Your essay’s biggest strength is its personal sincerity. Keep that, but combine it with:
a clearer thesis
more formal language
stronger paragraph structure
broader social reasoning
grammar and spelling corrections

If you revise in this way, your essay will become much more convincing as an argumentative piece.

===== ICLE-US-MICH-0035.1.txt =====
Feedback Report

Your essay presents a clear central idea: feminism can be understood as an important modern “discovery” because it has changed how society thinks about women and equality. This is an interesting argument, and your conclusion connects well to the introduction. The essay is also organized logically: history, then social image, then the future of feminism. For revision, focus mainly on accuracy, clarity, and development of support.

Strengths
Clear position: You argue consistently that feminism has had a major impact on modern life.
Good overall structure: Introduction, body paragraphs, and conclusion are easy to follow.
Thoughtful idea: Calling feminism a “discovery in progress” is original and gives the essay a strong ending.

Main issues to revise

A. Historical accuracy and precision
Some statements are too general or factually uncertain, which weakens your argument.

Examples:
“the 0th century” should be “20th century”
“women winning the right to vote in the 1800s” is inaccurate for the U.S.; women gained the constitutional right to vote in 1920
“The first significant equal rights movement in the U.S. was in the 70s” is also inaccurate; women’s rights movements were active much earlier, including the 19th century and again strongly in the 1960s–70s
“60 cents for every dollar” sounds unsupported and may be outdated

Advice: If you mention history or statistics, make sure they are correct and specific. If you are not sure, use more careful language, such as:
“By the mid-20th century…”
“In many places, women still earned less than men…”
“The women’s movement gained renewed momentum in the 1960s and 1970s…”

B. Stronger thesis and argument
Your essay has a main idea, but the thesis could be more direct. Right now, “feminism is a discovery” is interesting but somewhat abstract. Explain *why* it is a discovery.

For example, your thesis could say that feminism is a major discovery because it:
exposed long-standing inequality,
challenged traditional gender roles,
changed the way society represents women.

This will make your body paragraphs feel more connected to the thesis.

C. More development and examples
Your body paragraphs introduce good points, but they are brief. You often make a claim without explaining it fully.

For example:
In paragraph 2, you mention women’s roles in the 1950s and the Equal Rights Amendment, but you do not explain how these examples prove your argument.
In paragraph 3, you mention pornography and language, but this point needs more explanation. How exactly do they shape the image of women? Why is that important?

Advice: After each main claim, add 2–3 sentences of explanation or an example.

D. Word choice and tone
A few phrases sound awkward or imprecise.

Examples:
“modern day life” → modern life
“the woman in the home” → women at home or the ideal of women as homemakers
“housekeeping and tending the children” → doing housework and caring for children
“things as varying as” → things as varied as
“helps to form” → help form

Also, be careful with quotation marks. You put quotation marks around “discovery”, perhaps because you are using the word in a special way. That is fine once, but using quotation marks too often can distract the reader.

Grammar and sentence-level suggestions

A few important corrections:

“modern day life” → modern-day life or modern life
“towards” is okay, but be consistent; in American English, toward is more common
“it was still the most typical as well as the ideal” → it was still considered typical and ideal
“It’s only recently that we’ve ‘discovered’…” → better in formal writing: Only recently have we begun to recognize…
“how much things as varying as…” → how much things as varied as…
“the language we use helps” → the language we use helps
“Only when women have achieved equality with men, will there cease…” → remove comma: Only when women have achieved equality with men will there cease to be a need for feminism.

Suggested revision strategy
Correct factual errors first.
Rewrite the thesis to make your main argument more specific.
Expand each body paragraph with explanation and examples.
Improve formal style by revising awkward phrases and reducing unnecessary quotation marks.
Proofread carefully for grammar and punctuation.

Possible revised thesis
“Feminism can be seen as one of the most important discoveries of the 20th century because it revealed the depth of gender inequality, challenged traditional roles for women, and transformed the way society thinks about women’s identity and rights.”

You have a strong foundation and an interesting perspective. With more precise evidence and clearer explanation, this essay could become much more persuasive.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position and a consistent focus: you argue that care for the environment is one of the most important ideas of the 20th century. This is a strong topic, and your examples show that you understand environmental issues from several angles, such as nuclear waste, deforestation, recycling, energy conservation, and animal protection. The essay is easy to follow because each body paragraph discusses one aspect of the main idea.

What you do well

Clear central argument
Your thesis appears at the beginning and stays consistent throughout the essay. This is important in argumentative writing.

Good range of examples
You do not repeat the same point. Instead, you develop the topic through different environmental concerns, which gives the essay breadth.

Logical paragraphing
Each paragraph has its own topic, so the structure is already quite organized.

---

Main areas to improve

Make the thesis more precise
Your first sentence is understandable, but it is slightly awkward:

Original: “The idea I feel that has most significantly changed people's lives in the 20th century is that of caring for the environment.”
Better: “The idea that has most significantly changed people’s lives in the 20th century is the need to care for the environment.”

This version is more direct and formal.

Strengthen argument, not just description
At the moment, many paragraphs list environmental issues, but an argumentative essay should explain why this idea changed people’s lives. Try to connect each example back to daily life, government policy, or public attitudes.

For example, instead of only saying people are concerned about recycling, explain:
how recycling changed consumer behavior,
how governments created new laws,
how environmental awareness influenced education and business.

So the essay should answer: How did this idea actually change people’s lives?

Improve topic sentences and development
Some paragraphs begin well, but the support is sometimes too general. Try this pattern:

Topic sentence
Explanation
Specific example
Link back to thesis

For instance, in the recycling paragraph, you could mention specific changes such as recycling bins, public campaigns, or reduced landfill use.

Use more formal academic language
Several informal abbreviations should not be used in an academic essay:

& → and
w/o → without
b/c → because
2 → two

Also avoid conversational phrases like:
“we are all aware”
“basically”
“fixed everything”

Use more formal wording instead.

Correct grammar and word choice
Here are some important corrections:

“the importance of environment” → “the importance of the environment”
“countries that which have many weapons” → “countries that have many weapons”
“natural resources were never ending” → “natural resources were endless”
“what we use, needs to be replaced” → “what we use needs to be replaced”
“For example deforestation is a very big issue, many groups have formed…”  
  This is a comma splice. Better:
  - “For example, deforestation is a major issue, and many groups have formed to plant more trees.”
  - or split into two sentences.
“This links 2 issues, one of not producing much nonbiodegradable products & the other of reusing what we have.”  
  Better:
  - “This issue involves two goals: reducing the production of non-biodegradable products and reusing existing materials.”
“it also is beneficial” → “it is also beneficial”
“more considerable of our animal life” → “more considerate of animal life” or better “more concerned about animal life”
“we are heading in the right direction & hopeful by the 21st century…”  
  Better:
  - “we are heading in the right direction, and it is hoped that by the 21st century…”

Be careful with pronouns
You use we very often. This makes the essay sound less formal and sometimes too broad. You can replace we with:
people
society
many governments
the public

Conclusion needs a stronger finish
Your conclusion summarizes the essay, but the last sentence is too exaggerated: “fixed everything we have destroyed.” This sounds unrealistic. A better ending would be:

“Although many environmental problems remain, the growing awareness of environmental protection shows an important change in human values.”

---

Suggested revision priorities

Replace informal abbreviations and symbols.
Revise grammar and sentence structure, especially comma splices.
Make each paragraph explain how environmental awareness changed people’s lives.
Use more specific and formal examples.
Rewrite the conclusion in a more realistic and academic tone.

Possible revised thesis
You might use something like this:

“The idea of caring for the environment has significantly changed people’s lives in the 20th century because it has influenced public policy, personal habits, and attitudes toward natural resources, energy use, and animal protection.”

This thesis is stronger because it shows the main areas your essay will discuss.

You have a solid foundation and a clear idea. With more precise language and stronger explanation, this can become a much more effective argumentative essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear main idea: computers have changed people’s lives, especially by making storage, calculation, editing, and travel arrangements easier. You also use personal examples, which is good for an argumentative essay because they make your point concrete. The essay shows strong enthusiasm and a clear opinion. For revision, focus mainly on organization, grammar, word choice, and academic style.

Main strengths
Clear position: You clearly believe computers have had a major positive effect.
Relevant examples: Storing school information, balancing a checkbook, editing documents, and booking travel all support your claim.
Logical general direction: The essay moves from personal use to a broader social effect.

Main problems to revise

A. Organization and paragraph structure
Your essay has useful ideas, but some sentences are too long and contain several points at once. This makes the argument harder to follow.

Suggestions:
Begin with a short introduction stating your argument.
Put each major benefit in its own sentence or group of sentences:
  1. storing information
  2. processing/editing
  3. communication and travel arrangements
End with a stronger conclusion that summarizes why computers matter.

B. Academic style
This essay sounds too informal for academic writing.

Avoid:
symbols such as &, w/, w/out
conversational phrases like “Well”
vague phrases like “things of all sorts” or “this program seems to be extraordinarily helpful”

Use instead:
and, with, without
more formal phrasing: “Computers have greatly improved efficiency in daily life.”

C. Grammar and sentence errors
There are many grammar problems, especially with verb forms, word forms, articles, and sentence structure.

Wrong word form
“Computers have definitely effected peoples lives.”
  - effected should be affected
  - peoples should be people’s
  - Better: “Computers have definitely affected people’s lives.”

Verb tense/form
“my life has change” → “my life has changed”
“I had to this” → “I had to do this”
“would inevitably inundated” → “would inevitably be inundated”

Prepositions and articles
“in my computer” is usually “on my computer”
“applied to the this program” → “applied to this program”
“flew here form the US” → “flew here from the US”

Pronoun/reference problems
“How did I get there?” but then you say “I’m now here in Madrid.”
  - If you are already in Madrid, use “here,” not “there.”

D. Sentence clarity and run-on sentences
Many sentences are too long and connected only by commas. This creates run-on sentences.

For example:
> “I’m able to store almost all the information I need for school… I no longer need to keep these records but only to transfer them to my computer.”

This should be divided into shorter sentences:
“I can store almost all the information I need for school on my computer. Before I had a computer, my desk was cluttered with papers and records. Now I can organize these materials digitally.”

Shorter sentences will make your argument clearer and more persuasive.

Argument development
Your argument would be stronger if you moved beyond personal experience a little more. Right now, most evidence is based on your own life or assumptions.

For example, instead of saying:
“the transaction would seem impossible minus the aid of computers”

You could say:
“Airline reservations are much faster and more accurate because computer systems process schedules, payments, and passenger information efficiently.”

This sounds more objective and analytical.

Suggested revision strategy
Rewrite the introduction with a clear thesis.
   - Example: “Computers have significantly improved modern life by helping people store information, complete tasks more efficiently, and communicate or travel more easily.”
Separate long sentences into shorter ones.
Replace informal abbreviations with formal academic language.
Check grammar carefully, especially:
   - past participles (changed)
   - possessives (people’s)
   - prepositions (on the computer)
Strengthen the conclusion by restating the broader importance of computers.

Model of a possible revised thesis and topic sentences
Thesis: Computers have transformed everyday life by increasing efficiency, organization, and access to services.
Body paragraph 1: Computers help people store and organize large amounts of information.
Body paragraph 2: They save time by performing calculations and allowing easy editing of documents.
Body paragraph 3: They also improve communication and make services such as travel booking more convenient.

Final comment
You have a solid central idea and good real-life examples. Your next step is to make the essay sound more formal and grammatically accurate. If you revise for sentence control and academic style, your argument will become much stronger.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear position: you argue that the satellite is an extremely important invention because it allows people to witness world events immediately. This is a strong argumentative idea, and your example of the Tiananmen Square protests gives the essay emotional force. You also show ambition in connecting technology to politics, democracy, and global responsibility. These are good qualities in argumentative writing.

Main strengths

Clear thesis and strong opinion  
You make your position obvious from the beginning: the satellite is “the invention of the century.” This gives the essay focus.

Relevant example  
The China example supports your claim about live global communication. Using a real historical event makes the argument more persuasive.

Sense of significance  
You do not only describe technology; you explain why it matters. This is important in argument writing.

Main areas to revise

A. Improve the thesis and overall logic
Your main claim is interesting, but it is too absolute. Saying the satellite is the invention of the century may sound exaggerated unless you compare it carefully with other inventions.

Also, some ideas are not fully explained. For example, you say satellites make people “less tempted to do wrong,” but you do not prove this clearly.

How to revise:
Make the thesis more precise.
Explain exactly why satellites are more important than radio or television.
Show the connection between live broadcasting and political pressure more clearly.

Possible revised thesis:
> The communications satellite is one of the most important inventions of the modern age because it allows people around the world to witness events instantly, encourages international awareness, and increases pressure on governments to act responsibly.

This version is strong but easier to defend.

B. Organize the essay into clear paragraphs
Right now, the essay is mostly one long paragraph, which makes it harder to follow. A standard argumentative structure would help:

Introduction – present the claim  
Body paragraph 1 – explain what satellites do better than earlier technologies  
Body paragraph 2 – give the Tiananmen Square example  
Body paragraph 3 – explain broader effects on global politics and moral responsibility  
Conclusion – restate the argument

Each paragraph should focus on one main idea.

C. Use a more formal academic tone
Some phrases are powerful but too informal or emotional for an academic essay, such as:
“who wouldn’t want to help his neighbor?”
“keeps a watchful eye on all of us”
“China is placed down the block”

These expressions sound conversational. In academic writing, aim for a more balanced and formal style.

Example revision:
Instead of  
> Plus when China is placed down the block, who wouldn't want to help his neighbor?

You could write:  
> By making distant events feel immediate, satellite communication can increase international sympathy and encourage global responses to injustice.

D. Correct grammar, word choice, and spelling
There are many language errors that sometimes make the meaning unclear. This is the area that needs the most attention.

Here are some important corrections:

geo-centric satellite → geostationary satellite or simply communications satellite
evention → inventions
preceed → precede
invention possess → inventions possess
openned → opened
now the world functions → how the world functions
litterally → literally
Teniman Square → Tiananmen Square
sensorship → censorship
a like → alike
mor → more

There are also sentence-level problems:

Original:  
> As it happens- the most profound byproduct of the satellite.

This is not a complete sentence. You might write:  
> Its most profound contribution is the ability to show events as they happen.

Original:  
> Freedom US. the tank of injustice made its way into the lives of most of the world's people.

This sentence is unclear. You likely mean that the famous image of the student confronting the tank was seen worldwide. For example:  
> The image of a Chinese student standing in front of a tank brought the struggle for freedom into the homes of people around the world.

Advice about argument development
Be careful when making cause-and-effect claims. For example, saying that without satellites the democracy movement “would have been lost” is too strong. It is better to say that satellites helped the world witness the movement and increased international awareness.

Academic argument is stronger when it avoids unsupported certainty. Use phrases like:
helped to
made it possible for
contributed to
increased the likelihood that

Final suggestion
Your essay has a passionate and meaningful argument, but it needs clearer organization, more precise language, and careful editing. Focus especially on:
a more defendable thesis,
paragraph structure,
formal tone,
grammar and spelling correction.

You already have a strong central idea. If you revise for clarity and accuracy, this could become a persuasive essay.

Feedback Report

Your essay has a clear topic and a positive, persuasive tone. You argue that computers have changed both workplaces and education, and your main idea is easy to understand. You also organize the essay into separate paragraphs for business and student uses, which is a good start. For revision, I suggest focusing on three main areas: thesis and argument development, organization/coherence, and language accuracy.

Thesis and argument development

Your essay’s central claim is that computers have greatly influenced modern life. This is a strong topic, but the thesis should be more specific and direct. Right now, the introduction gives general background, but the main argument is not sharply stated.

For example, you could write a thesis like:

“Computers have transformed modern society by improving data management in organizations and by making learning and writing more efficient for students.”

This thesis is clearer because it tells the reader exactly what two main points the essay will discuss.

Also, some body paragraphs need more detailed support. You give examples such as libraries, hospitals, and students typing papers, but the explanation could go further. Ask yourself:
How exactly do computers save time?
Why are they better than older methods?
What specific benefits do students gain besides spell-check?

For instance, in the student paragraph, you could mention research on the internet, online learning platforms, educational software, and easier revision of essays.

Organization and coherence

Your essay has a basic structure, but transitions between ideas can be smoother. Some sentences feel disconnected or too general. Try to make each paragraph follow this pattern:

Topic sentence
Example
Explanation
Link back to the main argument

For example, your paragraph about companies starts well, but the library example is slightly confusing because a library is not exactly a “large company.” You could broaden the category to organizations and institutions instead of only companies.

Also, the fourth paragraph has useful ideas, but the sentence beginning “Computers can now also be used as great teaching instruments…” is unclear and grammatically difficult. Try breaking long sentences into shorter ones.

A possible revised structure:
Introduction with clear thesis
Computers help organizations store and manage information
Computers help students write, research, and learn
Conclusion: computers will continue to shape the future

Language accuracy and style

There are many good ideas in your essay, but grammar, spelling, and word choice errors sometimes make the meaning unclear. Below are some important patterns to revise.

A. Spelling
Please check these words:
definantly → definitely
accesible → accessible
previosly → previously
god send → godsend (usually one word)

B. Informal language
Avoid abbreviations in formal academic writing:
w/o → without
imp → important

C. Grammar and word form
Some phrases need correction:
“With respect to the people computers…” → awkward; better: “For ordinary people, computers have had a tremendous influence.”
“able to be filed into” → better: “stored in”
“They have enabled to students” → “They have enabled students”
“an incredible useful tool” → “an incredibly useful tool”
“they is clearly imp” → “they are clearly important”

D. Word choice
A few phrases are unclear or unnatural:
“the cosm graph in Mathematics” is difficult to understand. If you mean charts, graphs, or complex mathematical concepts, say that directly.
“paper writing” → better: “essay writing” or “writing papers”
“file large amounts of information” → better: “store large amounts of information”

E. Sentence control
Some sentences are too long or contain multiple ideas. Shorter sentences will improve clarity. For example:

Original:  
“Computers can now also be used as great teaching instruments from everything from Biology to understanding of the cosm graph in Mathematics.”

Possible revision:  
“Computers are also valuable teaching tools. They help students learn subjects ranging from biology to mathematics through visual and interactive programs.”

Conclusion

Your conclusion ends with an interesting idea about the future, but it should sound more formal and confident. Instead of “who knows where these machines will take us,” you could write:

“As technology continues to advance, computers will likely play an even more important role in work, education, and daily life.”

Final advice

For your next draft:
Write a more specific thesis.
Add deeper explanation and more concrete examples.
Improve paragraph unity and transitions.
Correct spelling, grammar, and informal expressions.
Use formal academic vocabulary.

You already have a solid main idea. With clearer support and more careful editing, this essay can become much stronger.

For example, your first body paragraph includes:
live global news
faster public reaction
shared information
cultural exchange
globalization

These are all relevant, but they should be organized more clearly. You could divide them like this:
Paragraph 1: Television spreads news instantly.
Paragraph 2: Television connects people across cultures.
Paragraph 3: Television entertains and teaches, although it can distort reality.

Improve paragraph structure
Each paragraph should have:
a topic sentence
supporting explanation
an example
a concluding sentence

For instance, your entertainment paragraph begins well, but the discussion of violence and reality could be developed with a specific example from TV drama or action programs.

Correct sentence structure and punctuation
Your biggest language issue is the run-on sentence. Several sentences are connected with commas, dashes, and semicolons in ways that make them grammatically incorrect or difficult to read.

Example:
> “While previously people had to wait days... now they can see it happening... this allows them...”

This should be broken into shorter sentences.

A clearer version:
> “Previously, people had to wait days or even weeks for news. Much of that information came from second- or third-hand accounts and was often distorted. Now people can watch events live and form their own judgments.”

This is easier to understand and sounds more academic.

Use a more formal academic style
Avoid informal punctuation and expressions such as:
“&” → use and
“well” in parentheses
“thru” → through
repeated conversational phrases like “for themselves, as it is happening”

Also, be careful with overgeneralizations such as:
“every person in the world can watch”
“everybody receives the same inflow of information”

These are too absolute. More accurate choices:
many people around the world
millions of viewers
in countries where television is widely available

Improve grammar and word choice
Here are some specific corrections:

“an invention of the 20th century” → better: “One of the most important inventions of the 20th century”
“this allows them to make” → if starting a new sentence, capitalize: “This allows them...”
“speak & act” → “speak and behave”
“through television entertainment” → “through television programs”
“various actions acted out to their ends” → awkward; try “various actions and their consequences”
“making life seem the good guy always wins” → “making it seem as if the hero always wins”
“sense of reality is not so distorted” → unclear; better: “television does not completely destroy people’s sense of reality”

Suggested revision strategy

Write a stronger introduction with a thesis that names your two main reasons.  
   Example:  
   *Television has significantly changed people’s lives because it provides immediate access to information and offers entertainment that can shape people’s understanding of society.*

Divide the body into clearer paragraphs.

Shorten long sentences and check each one for a subject and a verb.

Replace informal language with academic vocabulary.

Add a short conclusion that restates your opinion clearly.

Final comment

You have good ideas and a strong argumentative foundation. The main revision goal is clarity: organize the points more carefully, use shorter sentences, and adopt a more formal style. If you do that, your essay will become much more persuasive and effective.
